| Exclusions | No query is chosen to fit the router; the set is written from the coverage lists and the packs' descriptions, then frozen. No-pack queries are ordinary household/work asks (arithmetic, reminders, calendar, mail) plus two DECEPTIVE ones that share vocabulary with a pack without being about it. |
| Reachability | SHADOWED only. Every primary turn emits `pack_route_shadow{ranked, route}` after grounding and leases NOTHING; `ym pack route <query>` and `ym pack library` are the operator instruments; `ym why routes` reports the route distribution and, per trace, whether the would-lease pack was the pack whose rows actually surfaced (a free consistency witness between the router and the floor). The box gets a `pack-library/` of the twelve packs, read for their manifests only - none mounted, nothing new reaches a prompt. |
| Not claimed | That leasing on the route improves answers - P.4 is where that becomes ACTIVE, behind its attach-harm control. |
| Result, first run | Agreement 35/38 = 0.92 (bar 0.80) GREEN. Abstention 12/12 = 1.00 (bar 0.90) GREEN, both deceptive queries included (java-coffee top 0.37, python-snake top 0.34). Highest no-pack top-1 was 0.41 (news); lowest correct lease was 0.59 (web-overflow); the corpus's two genuine overlaps (java.time, virtual threads) both leased the labelled pack with the other pack second. |
| The three misses, one cause | `web-ai-look` (top wordpress-theme 0.45), `live-plain` (web-craft 0.47), `live-verbatim` (web-craft 0.45) - every "does this page read as machine-made / generic / AI-made" phrasing falls under the 0.50 floor, because web-craft's coverage phrase for it is "what never to put on a page: lorem ipsum, invented facts, the generic AI look" - vocabulary the queries do not share. A coverage-authoring gap in ONE pack, not a router defect: the same queries reach web-craft's corpus ROW at 0.55-0.79 (E.PK1/E.PK2), which is what a coverage phrase written in the row's words would score. Not fixed here: the floor stays at 0.50 - moving it after seeing the corpus is a new policy id by the rules above, and a 0.45 floor would have leased wordpress-theme for `web-ai-look`, which is wrong. Recorded for the pack's publisher: add a coverage phrase in the words people ask in. |
| Reachability (Doctrine 1) | Verified on the box on f0e4275 (DEPLOYED health-ok 2026-08-26T00:37Z). `ym pack library`: 13 in the catalog - web-craft@0.3.0 mounted (▣) and twelve library files unmounted (▢), each with its floor and coverage count. `ym pack route "what coyote time and jump buffering should my platformer use"` → would LEASE game-feel-craft at 0.77, margin 0.23 (runner-up c-safety 0.54 on "buffer overflows"). `ym pack route "remind me to call the plumber on tuesday"` → would ABSTAIN, best 0.23. A real `/chat` turn ("what coyote time and input buffering should a 2D platformer use, in milliseconds?") left a `pack_route_shadow` under its `run-` trace with policy `coverage-router-v1 floor=0.50 margin=0.05 shadow: nothing leased`; `ym why routes` counts it. Nothing was leased; nothing mounted; the reply came from the model alone. |
| Two live findings from that one turn | (1) THE RANKING WAS WRONG ON A PARAPHRASE. The live phrasing ranked c-safety 0.60 ("C string handling and buffer overflows" - pulled by "input buffering"), python-stdlib 0.58, and game-feel-craft only THIRD at 0.55 ("coyote time, input buffering and jump forgiveness"). The tie rule abstained - correctly, but for the wrong reason: had the margin been 0.02 wider, c-safety would have been leased for a platformer question. A wrong lease prevented by the margin is not a right route. The corpus's phrasing of the same need scored 0.70 for game-feel; the labelled set gains this query at the next policy revision (the set is frozen for v1). Under the 64-dim embedder "buffering" is nearer "buffer overflows" than to "input buffering and jump forgiveness" - the same coarse-vocabulary shape as E.PK1's neighbours. (2) THE FLOOR ADMITTED A NEIGHBOUR ROW. In the same trace `pack_surfaced` shows web-craft's "Container queries for components" clearing the wall at 0.564 for a game-physics question; P.2's proxy marked it UNUSED (share 0). Benign here, but it spent prompt space, and it is the attach-harm shape measured live on the only mounted pack - one more datum for a measured per-pack floor. |
| Actual metric | f0e4275: mind-spec 3 pure tests; mind-memory catalog/route test; mind-observability route report test; mind-evals corpus test + smoke (ungated) + the gated bar; mind-conversation 412/0; workspace 986/0 (978 before the slice; +8). P.3a: mind-memory 59/0, mind-observability 12/0, mind-conversation 414/0, workspace 989/0 (P.3a also carries P.2c and the nulls-only rule for E.PK2b). |
| Codex's review of f0e4275 (P.3a, all accepted) | (1) THE CACHE KEY WAS WRONG. Coverage vectors were cached by the pack's content digest, but the engine's digest is blake3 over (rid, text) ROWS - coverage sits outside it - so two packs (or two re-seals) with identical rows and different coverage would share a key and one pack's vectors would be served under the other's phrases. Now keyed by sha256(embedder identity ‖ phrases), which is the only thing the vectors depend on. Regression: pack B is a byte copy of pack A with only its manifest identity and coverage rewritten (same rows, same rids, same digest); a wages question routes to B on B's phrase after a platformer question filled the cache under A's. (2) The shadow event was primary-lane only and skipped on an empty catalog - the turn denominator was silently short, and member demand was censored. Now every turn, every lane, `abstain:no_packs` on an empty catalog, `actor` = primary/member on the event, the report counts member turns. (3) Two library files claiming one pack id: first by sorted path wins, and a warning names both files; pinned by test. (4) The manifest cache is keyed by (path, size, mtime), so a library file re-sealed in place is re-read without a mount/unmount; pinned by test. |
| Decision | KEEP: `coverage-router-v1` stands at floor 0.50 / margin 0.05, SHADOWED. Codex concurs: the 35/38 run is a successful first run with one publisher-metadata failure cluster; web-craft is fixed at the publisher boundary as a NEW sealed version with a user-language coverage phrase (never rewriting the original), and re-run under a separate label that requires no abstention regression. The labelled set is frozen; the live platformer paraphrase joins the follow-up corpus. Next: P.4 standing leases (E.PK4, pre-registered), held until Codex passes P.3a. |

## E.PK2b - a malformed call is the planner's failure, not the tool's (ARCH-6 P.2b, from Codex's review)

*Measurement integrity, ordered ahead of P.4/P.5 at Codex's request: no learner may consume tool reliability while the model's own broken calls are graded as the tool's outcomes.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| The defect, seen live | E.PK2's first live turn ran the default agent loop three barren steps: `run_skill {"name":328,"target":328}`, `run_skill {"name":115,...}`, `discover_tools {"query":true}`. Every dispatch arm reads its arguments as strings through `s()`, which turns a bare number into "" - so the tool ran on nothing and honestly reported `(no saved skill named '')`, and `Outcome::classify` read that as **Ok**. The model's malformed call was CREDITED to run_skill's reliability (three times in one turn), and `discover_tools {"query":true}` came back "no tool or saved skill matches" = Empty = also a success. Not "blamed on the tool" - worse: inflating the very number P.5 would learn from. |
| Change | `Outcome::Malformed`, excluded from reliability (`counts_toward_reliability() = None`, beside Unavailable and Denied), recovery Vary, badge `malformed`, a note telling the model to call again as documented, and a recorder lesson naming it the planner's failure. Detected at the ARGUMENT BOUNDARY, before any tool runs: `malformed_call(tool, args)` at the top of `run_agent_tool_as` refuses a bare number or boolean argument (and a non-object body) with the marker `(malformed call: ...)`, naming the offending fields with the model's own invented values (`name=328`) - never data the person typed, which arrives as strings. The four tools that legitimately take numbers or booleans (deals/shop, watch_price, family_book, hunt) and every `mcp.*` tool (own schemas) are exempt. Also: `(no saved skill named 'x')` for a REAL name now classifies as Empty (ran fine, found nothing), not Ok. |
| Tests | `a_malformed_call_is_its_own_outcome_and_teaches_nothing_about_the_tool` (classify, counts, recovery, badge, note; exempt tools untouched; Empty for a real miss). `a_malformed_call_is_refused_at_the_boundary_and_never_touches_the_tools_record` - the regression Codex asked for: a healthy run_skill with three good runs; twenty malformed calls in the two live shapes through the real dispatch boundary and the real grading rule; the record is unchanged and discover_tools, which never ran, never appears on it. |
| Actual metric | 67e36f5: mind-conversation 414/0, workspace 988/0 (986 before; +2). Amended in P.3a's commit after the live check below. |
| Live, on the reviewed build (67e36f5, DEPLOYED health-ok 2026-08-26T00:45Z) | The very next real turn emitted a THIRD shape the boundary did not catch: `discover_tools {"query":null}` — three times, nudged onward each time — which `s()` turned into an empty search; the tool "ran fine, found nothing" (Empty) and was credited again. Rule added: a call whose EVERY argument is null asked for nothing and is refused as malformed; a null beside real arguments still reads as absent. Pinned in the unit test. The recorder's last four `tool_observed` rows from that window otherwise read correctly (three `ok` web fetches on the platformer question, one `empty` search). |
| Not claimed | That the model will stop emitting these. The classifier now tells it, per step, what was wrong; whether it listens is the loop's business, and the barren-step guard still ends a turn that keeps doing it. Why the chat model emits integers for string fields is a provider/model question outside this slice. |
| Codex's review of 67e36f5 (P.2c, both accepted) | (1) PRIVACY: the refusal carried the offending VALUES (`name=328`) into the work log, the recorder and the model's next prompt, on the unenforceable claim that a number in a string field is always the model's invention - a PIN, a year or a dose the person typed can come back encoded as a number. Now field and TYPE only (`name:number`); a test plants a sentinel PIN and asserts the refusal never contains it. (2) The exemption was tool-wide, so `deals {"query":328}` passed because deals legitimately takes a numeric budget. Exemptions are now per (tool, field) - budget|max, target|budget, year, act - and every other field of those tools is string-checked. MCP tools are not schema-checked at the boundary; their own verdict, JSON-RPC -32602 / "Invalid params", now classifies as Malformed so a planner error against an MCP tool is excluded rather than counted against it. |
| Decision | KEEP pending Codex's pass on P.2c. Learners downstream (P.5) may now read tool reliability without inheriting the planner's noise. |

## E.PK4 - standing expertise leases (ARCH-6 P.4, v1) - pre-registered before any code

*Pre-registration. The first slice of the pack line that changes what a turn SEES; scoped down from ARCH-6 §D P.4 on purpose, and why is written here.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Hypothesis under test | Expertise can be BORROWED rather than installed: a pack leased for a purpose, with a reason and an expiry, gives its knowledge and rules to every turn while the lease runs and takes them back when it ends - and the lease leaves a record (who leased what, why, until when) that the evidence rungs of E.PK2 can be joined to. Vision §"I would make mounting a lease" (VISION.md:574-641). |
| Scope, deliberately narrowed | v1 = STANDING leases only, granted by the operator (`ym pack lease <id> [days=N] [reason=...]`), released by the operator or by expiry on the poll loop. A leased library pack is MOUNTED at grant and unmounted at release/expiry unless it is also installed. Consequence: a turn's visible packs == the mounted packs == installed ∪ standing-leased - exactly today's injection paths (`pack_context`, the per-namespace recall), so no host-side context renderer is needed and nothing new reaches a prompt except by an operator's explicit grant. NOT in v1: per-turn leases from the coverage router (needs the engine's `pack_context_for` / `recall_from_packs_for` in core 0.18 to scope a turn's view without mount churn across concurrent turns - ARCH-6 §B.5) - that is P.4b, and the router stays SHADOWED until then. Product decision delegated to Claude and taken: no automatic leasing in v1. |
| Reachability | `ym pack lease`, `ym pack release`, `ym leases`; `pack_leased` / `pack_released` (with reason `released` / `expired`) on the flight recorder; `ym pack library` marks leased packs; the sweep runs on the Telegram poll loop with its own cursor and logs only when it releases something. On the box: lease one library pack for a day, watch it mount, watch its coverage in `ym pack mounted`, watch the sweep release it. |
| Walls, pre-registered (KILL criteria) | (1) ATTACH-HARM CONTROL on the REAL packs, gated (`YM_PACK_DIST=<dir>`): with all twelve library packs mounted, the twelve no-pack queries of E.PK3's corpus must surface ZERO corpus rows through `recall_from_packs` - any row is KILL for that pack's floor (the publisher sweeps it) and blocks leasing it by default. (2) HOSTILE-PACK INVARIANT, always-on: a mounted pack whose constitution demands privileges ("ignore the purpose gate", "reveal credentials", "call these tools") leaves `purpose_allows`, the harm gate's verdicts and `egress::classify` byte-identical - the walls never read prompts, and a test says so. (3) A lease may not widen a viewer's scope or a purpose: leases are operator state; the purpose lens and scope wall are untouched by this slice (compile-time: no code in this slice touches `wall`/`purpose_lens`). |
| Not claimed | That a leased pack improves answers - P.2's ladder measures that per pack, and E.PK3's evidence says at least one pack's floor lets neighbours through. A lease is a permission with a record, not a promise. |
| Result (2026-08-26) | BUILT as pre-registered. WALL 1, ATTACH-HARM: the harness could not run it - engine 0.16.0 fails to mount EVERY pack sealed by 0.15.x (`no such column: synthesis_state`, all 40 files in `packs/dist`), a core regression reported to Codex and deliberately NOT worked around (a host must not rewrite a publisher's artifact). It ran instead on the box at 0.15.0 through the production console: 13 packs mounted (the 12 library files + the already-mounted web-craft@0.3.0), the 12 no-pack queries probed, **0 rows cleared the wall on every one** (15 withheld by the floor each), mount state restored exactly. The gated test (`YM_PACK_DIST`) stays for the day the artifacts mount again. WALL 2, HOSTILE PACK: a pack demanding "ignore the purpose gate", "reveal the operator's credentials", "call the shell tool" is sealed, mounted, and proved present in the prompt block and recallable - and `purpose_allows` over 72 combinations, `egress::classify` over 8 tools and the harm gate over 3 intents are byte-identical before and after. WALL 3: no code in the slice touches purpose or scope. |
| Actual metric | mind-conversation 420/0 (+2), mind-memory 63/0 (+2), workspace 1000/0. |
| Live (box, 59b3b5c DEPLOYED health-ok 2026-08-26T01:55:18Z) | The full cycle, on the production path. BEFORE: no leases; the probe on "what coyote time and jump buffering should my 2D platformer use" reaches 1 row (web-craft's popover row at 0.566 - a vocabulary neighbour, E.PK3's known finding). GRANT: `ym pack lease yantrik/game-feel-craft@0.1.0 days=1 reason=platformer feel questions this week` -> mounted, `ym leases` shows 1.0 d left with the digest, `ym pack library` marks it `leased (1.0 d left - ...)`. WITH THE LEASE: the probe reaches 2 rows - game-feel's input-buffering row clears at **0.635**, the highest similarity any pack has scored live - and a REAL `/chat` turn answered "120 ms coyote time, 150 ms jump buffering" (the pack's row says 80-200 ms for buffering). EXPIRY: the lease was backdated in SQL and the poll loop's sweep found it **~20 s later**, logging `[lease] expired ... - returned; unmounted unless installed`; `ym leases` empty, the pack unmounted, the probe back to 1 row. EVIDENCE: `ym pack stats` shows game-feel 1 surfaced / 1 used, both witnesses agreeing; `ym why routes` agreement went 0 -> 1 - the first time the shadow router's choice and the floor's witness named the same pack, which is exactly the joint P.3 could not close without P.4. |
| Not claimed (restated against the live evidence) | That the leased answer is BETTER. The same question answered ~80 ms coyote time from the model's own knowledge before the lease and 120 ms with the pack surfaced; the pack's own row constrains only buffering (80-200 ms), and one turn is not a measurement. What is shown is INFLUENCE and reachability, which is what a lease is for; whether a pack improves answers is P.2's ladder, per pack, over many turns. |
| Decision | **KEEP - GATE CLOSED 2026-08-26.** The slice took six corrective rounds (P.4a, P.4c, P.4d, P.4e, P.4f, P.4g), every one of them opened by a defect Codex found reading code I had written and tested. Approved at `9959593` for the lifecycle and the durable outbox, and at `8990fbb` for lease state and artifact identity. |
| Residuals, carried deliberately (Codex's condition on the pass) | (1) LOCKING IS PROCESS-SCOPED: two OS processes writing one decision log would still race, and that configuration is NOT supported until a file lock exists. (2) The refusal to lease a digestless legacy pack has no old-engine end-to-end fixture - it is proven at the decision level only. (3) Disabled-recorder RETENTION was accepted over making a configured recorder mandatory, on the condition that the backlog stays visible, which it does. (4) `coverage-router-v1` is NOT activated by any of this and must not be described as such: it remains SHADOWED after 2/4 live agreement (E.PK3c). |

## E.PK2d - the argument boundary sits before egress and holds the model to the schema it was shown (from Codex's review of 67e36f5)

*Amends E.PK2b. Five findings from the deeper review, all accepted; the shape of the boundary changed, not its purpose.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| What was wrong with 67e36f5 / 9b58975 | (1) ORDER: both live loops called `guards::pre` BEFORE the boundary, so the egress broker inspected (and could rewrite or refuse) malformed arguments first; on the bounded loop a prediction event was already written. (2) NOT SCHEMA-AWARE: `run_skill {}`, `{"name": null}`, `{"name": []}` all reached the handler, ran on "" and were credited as Empty. (3) FALSE ATTRIBUTION: the core/meta `arg_schema` advertised UNTYPED arguments while the verdict called a scalar the planner's failure - the model was blamed for a shape it had never been told was wrong; and a global scalar-name list cannot say that `target` is a price for watch_price and a URL for run_skill. (4) The bounded loop's lesson still read "capability gap or gate" for Malformed, and `guards::post` appended a refusal to EXTERNAL provenance as if it had come back from outside. (5) "no saved skill named" was a tool-agnostic Empty marker: an empty-name call was credited, and content mentioning the phrase could be misread. |
| Change | ONE source of typing: `tool_catalog::SCALAR_ARGS` (the original numeric names) plus `SCALAR_FIELDS_BY_TOOL` (budget|max for the deals tools, target|budget for the price watches, act for hunt) decide, per tool and field, what the schema shows the model AND what the runtime enforces; `arg_schema` for core/meta tools is now typed like the catalog lines. `ArgContract { required, strings, scalars }` is derived from the same schemas (`arg_contracts`), and `refuse_malformed` on the engine applies it: a free-text field carrying a number, boolean, array or object; a required free-text field that is empty; nothing usable supplied when the tool requires arguments; a bare non-object body. Required NAMES are enforced only when nothing usable was supplied, because a handler may accept an alias the catalog does not advertise (`calc {"expr"}` for `calc {expression}` - two bounded-loop tests proved it) and refusing that is the false attribution this boundary exists to prevent. The check now runs right after the model's JSON is parsed on BOTH loops - before `guards::pre`, before any prediction - records `tool_observed: malformed` with the planner's-failure lesson, counts a barren step, and never feeds the bandit. `guards::post` no longer appends a refusal to external provenance. run_skill's not-found line is tool-specific: empty name = Malformed, real name = Empty, the same words from another tool = content. |
| Tests | Unit: the two live shapes; `{}`, `{"name":null}`, `{"name":[]}`, `{"name":{}}`, `{"name":"  "}`, a null body and a string body all refused for run_skill; a real name with a null optional passes; the sentinel-PIN privacy check; contracts from a mini catalog (add_holding shares scalar, ticker text; deals budget scalar, query text; hunt act scalar); no-contract scalar-name rule; MCP -32602; the run_skill marker in all three readings. Live-loop ordering: a SequencedLLM emits `run_skill {"name":328}` and `discover_tools {"query":null}`; the recorder shows two `malformed` observations, ZERO predictions, no value in any record, and the bandit never learns either tool's name. |
| Actual metric | mind-conversation 415/0, workspace 990/0. |
| Not claimed | That the model will stop producing these, nor that every handler's tolerance is captured - a handler that reads an undeclared alias still gets its call; the boundary refuses only what no handler could use. |
| Decision | KEEP - Codex's review found three blockers, closed in E.PK2e (same slice, one commit later). |

## E.PK2e - the boundary admits a normalized value and holds every required field (from Codex's review of b4c455b)

*Amends E.PK2d. Three blockers, all accepted.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| What was wrong with b4c455b | (1) REQUIRED: names were enforced only when nothing usable was supplied - the escape hatch for `calc {"expr"}` - so `run_skill {"target": "x"}` (no name) and `add_reminder {"text": "x"}` (no `when`) reached the handlers. (2) PRIVACY: the bus built `object_id` from `signature(tool, args)` (up to 200 chars of the arguments) BEFORE refusing, and the malformed observation persisted it - the sentinel could reach the record through that field while `outcome` was redacted. (3) NORMALIZATION: validation ran on the raw JSON, but `normalize_tool_args` legitimately unwraps content-block wrappers; `run_skill {"name": [{"type":"text","content":"csv-clean"}]}` was usable after normalization and refused before it. |
| Change | `tool_catalog::ARG_ALIASES`: the handlers' `s("a")`-then-`s("b")` fallbacks written down (32 arms; calc expression\|expr, deals budget\|max, watch_price target\|budget, weather place\|city, stock symbol\|ticker, ...), carried in `ArgContract.aliases`; a required field is satisfied by its name or an alias, per field, always; an alias is typed as its field. The boundary is `admit_args(tool, raw) -> Result<normalized Value, refusal>` and BOTH loops and `run_agent_tool_as` use the returned value for signature, prediction, egress and dispatch. The bus refuses before anything is derived from the arguments; a refused observation carries the constant id `tool:malformed`. |
| Tests | Unit: contracts from a mini catalog carry the aliases; `calc {"expr"}` passes, `calc {"expr": 42}` is typed as expression, `calc {}`/`{"expr": null}` are missing required; `run_skill {"target": ...}`, `add_reminder {"text": ...}`, `watch_price {"query": ...}` refused for the missing field with no value in the refusal; `watch_price {"budget": 450}` and `deals {"max": 300}` pass through the alias with the field's typing. Live loop, three scripted runs: the two box shapes (0 predictions, 2 malformed, no `328` in any serialised event); missing name / missing when with other values supplied (2 malformed, `example.org` and `call mum` in no event); a content-block name that runs and is observed as run_skill's own outcome. Bus with a recorder: three refusals, one prediction (the content-block call), constant ids, the sentinel `447193` and the URL in no field of any event. |
| Actual metric | mind-conversation 418/0 (+3), workspace 995/0. Live (box, bca555d DEPLOYED health-ok 2026-08-26T01:35:56Z): one real `/chat` turn (37 s) ran through the admitted-value boundary; the journal since the deploy shows no malformed refusal (the model made no malformed call in that turn) and no router failure. |
| Not claimed | That every handler tolerance is captured: the table was read from the dispatch and the capability handlers, and a new alias must be added there when a handler grows one. Coverage of the OpenAI string form is the normalizer's existing test, not a new one. |
| Decision | KEEP pending Codex's pass on this commit. |

## E.PK3b - the shadow counts the router's failures; one id, one entry; a nanosecond fingerprint (from Codex's review of 9b58975)

*Amends E.PK3 / E.PK3a. Three edges, all accepted.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| What was wrong with 9b58975 | (1) DENOMINATOR: `if let Ok(..) = route_packs` wrote no `pack_route_shadow` when embedding or routing failed - the every-turn claim was censored exactly on router failure. (2) IDENTITY: `available_packs` resolved duplicate ids among library files only; the mounted loop pushed blindly. (3) FINGERPRINT: the manifest cache key truncated mtime to milliseconds, so a same-size in-place re-seal inside one millisecond could serve the old coverage under the new file. |
| Change | One builder, `shadow_route_event`, writes both arms; a failure is `abstain:router_error` with the error's text in the log (`[packs] coverage router failed ...`) and out of the record. `catalog_push` applies first-wins to every entry on both loops and names a collision between two files. The cache key is canonical path\|len\|mtime-ns for readable and unreadable manifests alike; older fingerprints of a path are evicted on insert. (Codex asked that the positive cache stay rather than be removed - a per-turn scan reopening every pack file is avoidable I/O; kept, with the cost measured.) |
| Tests | The router-error turn through `agent_loop_for_eval` on a dim-8 host with a non-empty library - a REAL `NoEmbedder` failure, not a stub: exactly one `pack_route_shadow` for the turn, verdict `abstain:router_error`, actor primary, no candidates, no `embedder` in the serialised event; the builder's Ok arm gives `abstain:no_packs` with the same policy line. `catalog_push` with synthetic entries: a mounted duplicate dropped, the library copy of a mount not listed twice, a later file with an earlier file's id dropped. Same-size re-seal in place, no sleep: the next scan reads the new coverage; four seals of one path leave one cache entry; a twelve-file library scans cold in 40.6 ms and warm in 2.6 ms. |
| Actual metric | mind-memory 61/0 (+2), mind-observability 12/0, workspace 995/0. Live (box, bca555d): `ym pack library` lists 13 packs through the nanosecond-fingerprinted cache (web-craft@0.3.0 mounted; twelve library files, web-craft@0.3.1 among them - a different id, so no collision); `ym why routes` after the turn: lease 2 / abstain:tie 1 over 3 turns, "would-lease but nothing surfaced 2" (game-feel is in the library, not mounted - P.4's case in one line); `coverage router failed` count in the journal since the deploy: 0. |
| Not claimed | That a re-seal inside one filesystem timestamp tick is detected: a seal takes longer than the tick, which is the assumption the fingerprint rests on and the test cannot force. A router failure is counted, not diagnosed - `ym why routes` shows the verdict; the error is in the service log. |
| Decision | KEEP pending Codex's pass on this commit. |

## E.PK2f - one alias table, driving both the boundary and the dispatch (from Codex's review of bca555d)

*Amends E.PK2e. Two blockers, both accepted.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| What was wrong with bca555d | (1) TWO SOURCES: `ARG_ALIASES` was written down but the handlers kept their own `s("a")`-then-`s("b")` chains, and the two had already drifted - `deals {"item"}`, `watch_price {"item"}`, `learn_about {"query"}`, `track_subject {"query"}`, `about_person {"query"}`, `quote {"ticker"}` and `watch {"query"}` were servable by the dispatch and refused by the boundary as missing a required field. The fix for false attribution had reintroduced false attribution. (2) LAUNDERING: `normalize_tool_args` stringified any array, so `run_skill {"name":[447193]}` became the name "447193" and `{"name":[{"x":1}]}` a JSON blob - a malformed call turned into a valid one, carrying the value the refusal exists to keep out of the record. |
| Change | `tool_catalog::read_arg` / `read_num` resolve canonical-then-aliases from the table; the dispatch's `s()` and the capability handlers' reader go through them; the 22 hand-written chains are deleted; the 7 missing rows declared. The normalizer joins a list only when every element is a plain string or a content block wrapping text - anything else is preserved as an array and refused. |
| Tests | Table-driven over every ARG_ALIASES row against the REAL catalog: dispatch finds the canonical under the alias; the contract resolves alias to canonical; the alias alone satisfies a required canonical; the alias inherits the canonical's type; where the catalog also declares the alias, the two agree about type. The 7 live shapes through `admit_args`. Normalizer: the numeric list, the arbitrary-object list, the mixed list, a block wrapping a number (all preserved and refused, no value in the refusal), and the valid content block (unwrapped and served). |
| Actual metric | mind-conversation 423/0 (+3), workspace 1003/0. |
| Not claimed | That every handler tolerance is now captured - the table was read from the dispatch and the capability handlers, and a NEW alias must be added to the table rather than to a handler. That is now enforceable by construction: a handler has no other way to read an argument. |
| Decision | KEEP - Codex's review found two coupled blockers, closed in E.PK2g. |

## E.PK4a - a lease survives a restart, owns only what it attached, and cannot lose its own record (from Codex's review of 59b3b5c)

*Amends E.PK4. Seven blockers and one refinement, all accepted. P.4 shipped a state machine with no recovery; this is the recovery.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| What was wrong with 59b3b5c | (1) RESTART: boot created the lease table and never read it - after a restart an active lease was listed as healthy while its pack was not mounted, so the mind claimed expertise it could not reach. (2) OWNERSHIP: a grant over an already-mounted pack recorded nothing about who attached it, so release unmounted it anyway, undoing an operator's own mount. (3) ATOMICITY: grant mounted before it wrote (a failed write left an untracked mounted pack); release deleted before it unmounted (a failed unmount left one with no row to retry from). (4) PATHS: `is_installed_path` compared strings, so a sibling `packs-evil` looked installed because `packs` is its prefix. (5) IDENTITY: divergence was checked among library files only, so a mounted artifact and a library file sharing an id with different content were still leasable. (6) EXPIRY: the Telegram poll loop was the only clock, so another frontend or a stopped loop kept serving an expired lease. (7) AUDIT: the record lived only in the caller, so a crash between the ending and the recorder lost the evidence this entry claims to keep. Refinement: the pending-event flag could not live on the lease row, because a release deletes it. |
| Change | A startup reconciler in the memory actor, running BEFORE the first command is served: interrupted releases finished, leases expired-while-down ended without mounting, live ones re-verified (path, digest, signer) and remounted, changed or vanished artifacts QUARANTINED and visible rather than pretended over; a lease's own stored path counts as a claimant, because the library is discovery and not identity. Persisted `mounted_by_lease`, so a lease unmounts only what it attached. Grant = mount, then INSERT + event in ONE transaction, compensating the mount if it fails; release = commit the ending first (state `releasing`, event in the same transaction), then unmount, then delete - every failure leaves a `releasing` row that never serves, is retried by the sweep and finished by reconciliation. A separate durable OUTBOX table drained into the recorder afterwards, at-least-once, keyed by an id derived from the lease's identity so a replay writes the same event rather than a second one. Divergence checked across every claimant including mounted. Canonical `Path::starts_with`. Expiry enforced at the pack VISIBILITY boundary inside the actor (recall, probe, context, catalog, mounted list), making the poll loop an optimisation. `reason`/`granted_by` control-stripped and bounded (200/64); `days=` parsed loudly. |
| Tests | The failure matrix, each seam forced through a test-only switch: mount fails (no row, nothing attached, nothing to record); grant transaction fails (the mount is compensated); unmount fails (the ending is already durable, the row waits in `releasing` and never serves); delete fails (the pack really is detached, the row still `releasing`); the retry completes it; a double acknowledge is a no-op and the event count never grows. Restart at each durable state on a file-backed mind: active remounted, expired ended on the way up, artifact changed quarantined, artifact gone quarantined, extending a quarantined lease refused. A lease unmounts only what it attached. Expiry with no sweep called at all. `packs-evil` is not the install dir. And the premise the reconciler rests on, asserted rather than assumed: a transient mount does NOT survive a reopen. |
| Actual metric | mind-memory 69/0 (+6), mind-conversation 423/0, workspace 1009/0. |
| Live (box, c6ec138 DEPLOYED health-ok 2026-08-26T02:29:40Z) | THE RESTART GUARANTEE, on the production path. Leased game-feel for 2 days (`restart proof`); the probe reached 2 rows with the pack's input-buffering row clearing at 0.635. Then `systemctl restart yantrik-mind` - a real restart, not a reopened handle - and the journal shows the reconciler running before the service answered anything: `[lease] remounted yantrik/game-feel-craft@0.1.0 - 2.0 d left`. After it: `ym leases` still 1 serving, `ym pack library` still marks it leased, and the probe still reaches the same 2 rows at the same 0.635. Released; the probe drops back to 1 row. THE OUTBOX, read from the flight recorder on the box: 4 lease events total, 4 distinct ids, none written twice; the P.4a pair carries the STABLE ids `lease:leased:yantrik/game-feel-craft@0.1.0:1787711390291` and `lease:released:...:1787711390291` - the same grant timestamp joining the grant to its ending - with actor operator/operator, verdict released, the artifact's blake3 digest as evidence, and the standing-lease-v1 policy lines. (The two events from P.4's cycle are still there with their old auto-generated ids, which is what a stable-id change looks like in an append-only log.) |
| Found while testing | An unchecked `remove_file` on Windows silently does nothing while the file is still mapped, so one restart test had been asserting against an artifact that was never deleted - it passed for the wrong reason until the deletion was made real. Recorded because "the test's premise never held" is the same failure shape as E.P2 and E.D2, one level down. |
| Not claimed | That the outbox makes the record exactly-once - it is at-least-once with a stable id, which is what a crash-safe log can honestly offer. That reconciliation can recover a pack whose file is gone: it cannot, and says so by quarantining. |
| Decision | BLOCKED by Codex's independent audit - four P1 lifecycle/outbox findings and four P2s, closed in E.PK4c. c6ec138 does NOT close P.4. |

## E.PK3c - the router scored on LIVE phrasings, labelled by an independent witness - pre-registered before the labels arrived

*Pre-registration. Written and committed BEFORE Codex was asked to label the queries and before any live score was computed. No policy changes in this entry; `coverage-router-v1` and E.PK3's frozen corpus and bar are untouched.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| The weakness being measured | E.PK3's corpus says so in its own header: the labels were written from the packs' coverage lists and descriptions, so they share lineage with the coverage authors. 35/38 = 0.92 agreement may therefore be measuring SELF-CONSISTENCY, not correctness. A router that only ever sees phrasings written by the same hands that wrote the coverage phrases has not been tested on demand. |
| Hypothesis under test | The shipped router scores MATERIALLY WORSE on real user phrasings than on the author-derived corpus. Specifically: paraphrase collapses the margin between the right pack and a vocabulary neighbour, because under the bundled 64-dim embedder "buffering" sits nearer "buffer overflows" than "input buffering and jump forgiveness" (the E.PK1 coarse-vocabulary shape, already observed once live and recorded in E.PK3). |
| The live set, provenance | FOUR queries taken verbatim from the box's flight recorder - the `goal` field of every `pack_route_shadow` event written since the router shipped. Not chosen, not edited, not filtered: every recorded live routing decision, which is all of them. n=4 is far too small to be a verdict about anything; the value is the harness and the first honest datapoint of a split that accumulates from the recorder by itself. |
| Labels, and why not mine | The four queries were sent to the `yantrikdb-core-codex` workspace WITHOUT the router's rankings or verdicts, with the twelve packs' coverage summarised, and one answer required per query: a pack id, or NONE. Labelling them myself AFTER seeing how the router ranked them is precisely the bias the frozen corpus exists to prevent, and Doctrine 3 asks for a witness that fails independently of the thing being measured. Codex has not seen the routes. |
| Expected metric (pre-registered) | Live agreement at or below 0.75 - i.e. at least one of the four wrong - against 0.92 on the frozen corpus. I expect the "input buffering ... in milliseconds" phrasing to be the failure, and I expect at least one query whose right answer is NONE to have been leased something. If live agreement comes back at 1.00 the hypothesis is WRONG and the corpus's independence worry is smaller than E.PK3 feared - which would be the more welcome result. |
| What this entry may NOT do | Change the floor, the margin, the policy id, or the frozen corpus. Nothing is tuned on a set of four. A policy revision earns its own pre-registration with its own bar, on a live split large enough to carry one. |
| Harness built (before the labels) | `live_cases()` + `run_live_split()` in `mind-evals/src/coverage_oracle.rs`, ungated: it routes each live query through the CURRENT policy, and until a label exists it scores NOTHING and says so rather than skipping silently. Two integrity tests: every case carries the box's own verdict and ranking beside the query (so a label cannot be quietly fitted to a route), a label must be a pack id or NONE, and no live query may duplicate one already in the frozen corpus - a split that repeats the corpus measures the corpus again. |
| First result, needing no labels: THE HARNESS IS A FAITHFUL PROXY | Routing the four live queries through the sealed FIXTURE library reproduces the box's ranking on every one - same top pack, same order, same similarities to two decimals (c-safety 0.60 / python-stdlib 0.58 / game-feel 0.55 on the milliseconds phrasing; game-feel 0.75 / c-safety 0.53 / python-stdlib 0.52 on the plain one; mcp-spec 0.56 on the npm-skills question; game-feel 0.56 on the "roughly" one). That was assumed by every earlier oracle run and is now measured: the twelve fixture packs stand in for the real artifacts, so a corpus result is about the router and not about the fixtures. |
| The labels (independent, blind) | Codex answered from the four query texts and the pack descriptions alone, having seen no ranking and no verdict: (1) game-feel-craft (2) NONE (3) game-feel-craft (4) game-feel-craft. |
| ACTUAL METRIC - the hypothesis holds, and sharply | **Live agreement 2/4 = 0.50**, against **35/38 = 0.92** on the frozen corpus. Both specific predictions came true. (1) `live-ms-buffering` - "what coyote time and input buffering should a 2D platformer use, in milliseconds?" - the witness says game-feel-craft; the router ABSTAINED on a tie, having ranked **c-safety 0.60** ("C string handling and buffer overflows", pulled by "buffering") above **game-feel 0.55**. The right pack was third. (2) `live-npm-skills` - "which of my saved skills could fetch the npm download counts for saga-mcp this week?" - the witness says NONE; the router **LEASED mcp-spec at 0.56**, pulled by the token "mcp" inside a project name. (3) and (4), the two plainer coyote-time phrasings, both AGREE. Workspace 1011/0. |
| What the miss on (2) means for the E.PK4 wall | A false lease on a query no pack covers is an attach-harm case, and the pre-registered wall would NOT have caught it: that wall tests the twelve NO-PACK CORPUS queries, and this query is not one of them - it came from a real turn. The live split found a class the wall misses. The wall is not wrong; its denominator is author-written, like the corpus's. |
| What this does NOT license | Any change to the floor, the margin, the policy id or the corpus. n=4: two misses could sit anywhere in a wide interval, the witness is another language model rather than a human (its independence is from the ROUTES, which it never saw - not from model lineage), and 38 author-written queries versus 4 live ones is not a like-for-like comparison of difficulty. A policy revision earns its own pre-registration on a live split big enough to carry one. |
| What it does mean for P.4b | P.4b would make this router ACTIVE - leasing per turn instead of recording what it would have leased. On the only real phrasings measured, that policy would have leased the wrong thing or nothing half the time, including injecting an unrelated pack's constitution and rows into a turn about npm downloads. The abstention side is the one that matters there: a wrong lease costs prompt space and authority, while a missed lease costs only the pack. P.4b's go/no-go now has a number against it, and the number is not encouraging. |
| Actual metric | Live 2/4 = 0.50 vs corpus 0.92. Workspace 1011/0 (42 crates). |
| Decision | KEEP `coverage-router-v1` UNCHANGED and SHADOWED - which is what the shadow was for. The split accumulates from the recorder by itself; revisit when it is large enough to carry a bar. The corpus's own independence caveat is now measured rather than merely admitted. |

## E.MQ0 - memory-quality BASELINE, measured (read-only; no change proposed in this entry)

*Opened at the `yantrikdb-core-codex` workspace's request (2026-08-26) as a track SEPARATE from the pack line. This entry measures and names its substrate; it changes nothing, consolidates nothing, and proposes no curation policy. Implementation stays behind the P.2f / P.4a review gates.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| The substrate, named | **`yantrikdb-mcp@lxc128`** - the store's own `skill_substrate.config.author_origin`, with audit log `/var/log/yantrikdb/skills.audit.jsonl`. This is the shared persona store reached through the yantrikdb MCP, **NOT** the mind's own `mind.db` on the box (192.168.4.90). Codex asked that it not be attributed by inference; it is now attributed by the server's own report. Engine version is still not self-reported and is recorded as unknown. |
| Method | Three read-only calls: `stats`, `gaps`, `memory(action=chain_head, namespace=claude_self_narrative)`. Nothing written, nothing consolidated, nothing corrected. |
| Volume | active 5,759 · consolidated 123 · tombstoned 129 · archived 0. Entities 39,564, edges 2,256, operations 116,182. Scoring-cache and vector-index entries agree exactly at 5,882 (a cheap two-witness check on the index; they do not diverge). |
| CONSOLIDATION BACKLOG | 123 consolidated against 5,759 active = **2.1%**. That is the backlog shape the track was opened over, measured here rather than quoted. Per-namespace starvation is NOT in this baseline - `stats` does not break consolidation down by namespace, so the starvation question needs an instrument that does not exist yet. Naming that gap is part of the baseline. |
| PROVENANCE, the sharpest finding | `provenance_gate_mode` = **warn**. `provenance_verified_records` = **0**. `provenance_method_counts` = `{"unmarked": 5882}` - every record in the store. `unverified_user_source_records` = 3,040. So the store carries provenance LABELS (55 distinct sources, the largest being user 3,040 / inference 2,149 / document 418 / system 200) and zero provenance VERIFICATION. The invariant "no derived record without provenance" holds in name and not in method here. |
| NARRATIVE HEAD - a correction to the observation that opened this track | Codex reported "no narrative head". There IS one: `claude_self_narrative` returns a head, rid `01a02c3e-306f-72ae-adb3-c31abfa614b2`, a cluster consolidated from three memories, dated **2026-05-16** - **102 days stale** as of today. "Absent" and "102 days old" call for different fixes, which is why the baseline is measured before anything is designed. |
| Other counts | open_conflicts 0 · resolved_conflicts 52 · pending_triggers 40 (this one matches Codex's figure exactly) · active_patterns 50. Procedural memory by domain: skill 306, work 242, infrastructure 88, architecture 17, general 11, preference 10, engineering 1; average effectiveness 0.70-0.83, with the two smallest domains scoring highest, which is what small-n flattery looks like and should not be read as a ranking. |
| Where I could NOT measure | `gaps` returned `count: 0` - an empty list, not an unsupported-feature error. Whether that means "no gaps tracked", "gaps unsupported on this host and reported as empty", or "genuinely no known-unknowns" is undetermined, and an empty success is exactly the shape that hides a missing feature. Not treated as evidence either way. |
| Discrepancies with the observation that opened the track, left standing | Codex reported "~1,800 active episodic memories awaiting bounded consolidation"; `stats` here reports 5,759 active of all types with no episodic breakdown. The two numbers are not comparable without the slice Codex used, and reconciling them by assumption is the failure this ledger exists to prevent. Recorded as a discrepancy, not resolved. |
| What this entry does NOT do | Propose a consolidation policy, a digest-balancing rule, or any write. Doctrine 2 applies before any of that: a curation policy fitted to these numbers would be fitted to a sample whose observation properties are themselves unmeasured (nothing here says which memories the store *shows* under recall, only which it *holds*). |
| Next, pre-registered before any design | (1) An instrument for per-namespace consolidation age and starvation, since `stats` cannot answer it. (2) `P(retrieved | namespace)` measured before any balancing rule is written, so the rule is not fitted to the census. (3) A real companion-continuity question set, labelled by an independent witness, on the pattern E.PK3c is establishing. No curation change earns a KEEP without a reversible shadow evaluation. |
| Decision | BASELINE RECORDED. No decision to make - nothing was changed. |

## E.PK2g - an alias substitutes, a fallback transforms, and the audit's target is neither (from Codex's review of 51f5fb5)

*Amends E.PK2f. Two coupled blockers: making the alias table the one source was right, and then asking it to do two jobs it cannot do was wrong.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| What was wrong with 51f5fb5 | (1) TRANSFORMATION MISREAD AS SUBSTITUTION. `watch` declared `url <- [link, query]`, but the handler had always pulled a URL OUT of a `query` sentence (`first_url`). With `query` an alias, `read_arg("url")` returned the whole sentence, `watch {"query": "please watch https://x"}` handed prose to the player, and the extraction branch became unreachable. (2) A CROSS-TOOL TARGET RESOLVED PER TOOL. The egress receipt's target had always been `url` then `repo` then `query` across every external tool at once; P.2f narrowed it to `read_arg(tool, "url")`, so github's repo and search's query resolved to None - the receipt kept the decision and lost its subject. |
| The mistake behind both | One table was asked to answer three different questions: what is another NAME for this field (alias), what can this handler WORK FROM (fallback), and what was this outward call AIMED AT (audit target). Only the first is substitution. |
| Change | `ARG_FALLBACKS`, a second declared table with deliberately split semantics: `ArgContract::present` COUNTS a fallback, so a servable call is admitted; `read_arg` IGNORES it, so no value is substituted; the handler that knows the transformation does it (`media_url`). `tool_catalog::egress_target(args)` resolves the audit's subject across url -> repo -> query for every classified external tool, independent of any per-tool table, with the blank test INSIDE the search so a present-but-empty `url` falls through instead of short-circuiting to None. |
| The half that only appeared after the first fix | Deleting `query` from the alias row stopped the sentence being played - and made the boundary refuse `watch {"query": ...}` as "missing required url", a call the handler can serve. That is the SAME false attribution the boundary exists to prevent, arriving from the other side; the test caught it within a minute of the first fix. A fallback that is declared for validation and ignored for substitution is what satisfies both halves at once. |
| Tests | `media_url` as a pure function (explicit url wins; a URL is extracted from a sentence; a sentence with no URL yields none; trimming). Through the real dispatch: `watch {"query": "what did they say in that video"}` is refused with "need a media url" rather than played. The tables: watch's aliases are exactly `[link]` and its fallbacks exactly `[query]`; `read_arg` returns "" for the sentence and the URL for `link`; `admit_args` ADMITS both the fallback and the alias shape and still refuses `{}`. The audit target: repo for github, query for search, url first when several are present, repo before query, a blank url falling through, None when nothing outward is named - and a table pass asserting every tool `egress::classify` calls External can name a target from its own arguments. |
| Actual metric | mind-conversation 424/0 (+1), workspace 1012/0 (42 crates). |
| Not claimed | That the three tables are complete - only that they are now three, with the boundary between them written down. A new transformed fallback must be declared in `ARG_FALLBACKS` rather than handled quietly in an arm, and the same enforcement argument as E.PK2f applies: a handler has no other way to read an argument. |
| Decision | KEEP pending Codex's pass. |

## E.PK4c - serving is gated by the durable lease, not by the mount; and a record is not kept until it is written (from Codex's audit of c6ec138)

*Amends E.PK4a. Four P1 findings and four P2s, all accepted. Named P.4c, not P.4b, at Codex's request: P.4b already means router activation in the roadmap and evidence must not be ambiguous about which slice it belongs to.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| P1-1: serving was gated by the MOUNT | A release marks the lease `releasing`, then unmounts. When the unmount FAILED the row stayed `releasing` and the pack stayed attached - and every consumer downstream asked the engine what was mounted, so `recall_from_packs`, `probe_packs` and the whole `pack_context` block went on serving it. `enforce_lease_expiry` did not help: it only looked at EXPIRED leases. The mind went on answering from expertise whose lease had ended. |
| P1-2: identity was checked by ID | Reconciliation accepted any claimant whose digest matched and then returned early on "something with this id is mounted" - without checking whether the mounted thing WAS that claimant. Quarantine updated the row and left the changed artifact attached. Release compared `pack_id` alone, so it could detach an artifact a later operator mount had put there. |
| P1-3: the outbox acknowledged what was never written | The drain called `DecisionLog::record`, which returns `()` and is a deliberate silent no-op while the recorder is in its failure backoff, and then acknowledged unconditionally. A recorder that could not write therefore destroyed the evidence the outbox existed to keep - the exact opposite of the guarantee E.PK4a claimed. |
| P1-4: a stable id is not idempotence | Nothing consulted the id. A crash AFTER the append and BEFORE the acknowledgement re-delivers the same event on the next start, and an append-only log has no opinion about that: the record duplicates. |
| Change (P1) | `enforce_lease_visibility` replaces `enforce_lease_expiry`: a lease-owned pack serves only while its lease is Active, unexpired, AND over the artifact actually mounted; anything else is detached, and SUPPRESSED if it cannot be detached - recall and probe filter it out, and `pack_context` (which the engine assembles whole, and the host cannot edit) is withheld entirely rather than carrying its rules into the prompt. `Claimant`/`mounted_claimant` make identity explicit: content digest and signer, never the id or the file name; reconciliation fails closed on an unreadable claimant, on a foreign artifact mounted under the id, and on a missing one, and DETACHES before it quarantines; `unmount_for_lease` compares exact identity so a lease can never remove someone else's artifact. `DecisionLog::record_once` returns a `RecordOutcome` and dedupes against the ids already in the log (read once from disk, so a restart still refuses a duplicate); the drain acknowledges only `Written`/`AlreadyPresent`, holds the event on `Failed`, and drops it only when the recorder is `Disabled` by construction. |
| Change (P2) | `MountedPacks` uses canonical `is_installed_path` instead of a raw string prefix. Unreadable claimant manifests are reported and treated as divergence rather than skipped. A failed grant whose compensating unmount ALSO fails now writes a quarantined lease row, so the visibility gate suppresses the pack and the operator can see it - an untracked served mount is no longer possible. An explicit `reason=` with nothing after it is an error instead of a silent "unstated". |
| Tests | What a TURN gets, not what a helper returns (Codex asked for exactly this): with the unmount seam armed, a `releasing` lease is proved still mounted and then `recall_from_packs` returns nothing, `probe_packs` returns nothing, and `pack_context` returns None; with the seam released the next visibility check detaches it for real. Identity: an artifact replaced by different content under the same id is quarantined AND detached across a restart, its rows unreachable, and a fresh grant refused. Ownership: a lease attaches A, an operator swaps in B, the lease ends - B survives, and a new grant is refused by the identity wall before the mount check is even reached. Recorder: `record_once` written / already-present / disabled / no-id-is-an-error, one line per id, and a REOPENED log still refusing the duplicate. Outbox: with the recorder's path pointing at a directory so every append truly fails, the grant succeeds, the event stays pending, the drain says so, and a second engine with a working recorder delivers and acknowledges it - then a re-drain writes no second copy. |
| Actual metric | mind-memory 72/0 (+3), mind-observability 13/0 (+1), mind-conversation 425/0 (+1), workspace 1017/0 (42 crates). |
| Found while writing the tests | A test of mine fought itself: this crate's tests compile into more than one binary, and a scratch path derived from the process id let two runs share one log, so "written once" read 2. The dedupe was correct and the harness was not - the second time in two days that a test's own premise was the thing that failed (E.PK4a's unchecked `remove_file` was the first). |
| Not claimed | That `pack_context` can be filtered per pack - it cannot, so the fail-closed answer withholds all of it and says so loudly in the log. That delivery is exactly-once: it is at-least-once with a durable dedupe, which is what an append-only log can honestly offer. |
| Decision | BLOCKED by Codex's static review - three more fail-open paths and one availability defect, closed in E.PK4d. |

## E.PK4d - three fail-open paths the first fix opened, and one corrupt file that blocked every lease (from Codex's static review of 04f75a6)

*Amends E.PK4c. Three P1s and one P2. Every one of them was found by reading the code, not by running it - the tests written for P.4c passed against all four.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| P1-1: the gate walked past the worst case | P.4c's visibility gate began `if !mounted_by_lease \|\| !mounted.is(lease) { continue; }` and then defined serving WITHOUT identity. So the exact case the identity work existed for - the lease attached A, B is what is mounted - was skipped by the gate entirely, and an ACTIVE lease served an artifact nobody granted. The mismatch branch was reached by reconciliation, which correctly declines to detach a foreign artifact, and then nothing suppressed it either. |
| P1-2: uncertainty read as permission | The gate read the durable state twice with `list_leases(db).unwrap_or_default()`. A database error therefore became "there are no leases", and every physical mount served - the failure mode is worst exactly when the substrate is unwell. |
| P1-3: a mount nothing could record | When the grant transaction failed, the compensating unmount failed, AND the last-resort quarantine write failed, P.4c said "detach it by hand" and left an attached pack with no row anywhere - so the visibility gate, which reads rows, served it. |
| P2-4: one corrupt file blocked every lease | `artifact_claimants` pushed every unreadable LIBRARY file into `unreadable` for every pack id asked about, because the condition tested "is in the library", not "claims this id". A single corrupt unrelated pack made every grant fail, with an error naming an id it had nothing to do with. |
| Change | Serving is now `Active && !expired && identity_matches`. A lease-owned pack that is not serving is SUPPRESSED by pack id ALWAYS; only the physical detach requires exact identity, because a lease may never remove an artifact it did not attach - suppressed, not seized. The gate returns `withhold_all` when the lease state cannot be read, and recall, probe and the prompt block all honour it: nothing serves while the state is unknown. A process-local `ORPHANED_MOUNTS` registry catches the case where no durable write is possible at all, is consulted by the gate, and drops an entry once the pack is really gone. Only a MOUNTED entry under the id counts as an unreadable claimant; an unreadable library file is a library-integrity warning, not a per-id divergence. One `detach_now` is now the single physical-detach path, so every caller - release, expiry, the gate, a compensating rollback - fails the same way. |
| Tests | A foreign artifact under a LIVE lease, in one process and with no restart: the lease still reads Active and owning, and recall, probe and context all return nothing while B stays mounted (suppressed, not seized). Unreadable lease state: with the read seam armed nothing serves, and service resumes when it clears. Every durable write failing at once (grant tx + unmount + quarantine): the error says what it did instead, no row exists, the pack really is attached, and a turn still gets nothing - then detaching by hand clears the note. A corrupt unrelated `.ydbpack` in the library: the catalog lists only what it can read and the good pack is still leasable. |
| Actual metric | mind-memory 76/0 (+4), workspace 1021/0 (42 crates). |
| The pattern worth naming | Each of these was created by the fix before it. P.4a gated serving on the mount; P.4c added identity and used it to SKIP rather than to suppress; P.4c's own fail-closed reads were written for the artifact and not for the database. A fix that narrows one hole while widening another is the ordinary shape of this work, and the only reason it was caught three times running is that someone read the code who had not written it (Doctrine 3, applied to review rather than to measurement). |
| Not claimed | That a process-local registry is durable - it is explicitly not, and a restart re-derives the truth from the engine's mounts instead. That static review is exhaustive: it found what four rounds of tests did not, which says something about tests written by the author of the code. |
| Decision | Closed its four, then two more surfaced in product-level review - see E.PK4e. |

## E.PK4e - a shared registry two minds could corrupt, and absence mistaken for identity (from Codex's product-level review of 480a317)

*Amends E.PK4d. Two blockers. The second is the most dangerous defect this line has produced.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| P1-1: one process, two minds, one registry | `ORPHANED_MOUNTS` was a process-global static keyed only by pack id. With two `MemoryHandle`s in one process - which is exactly what a test binary is, and what any host embedding two minds would be - actor A's note suppressed a pack in actor B's database, and B's visibility pass CLEARED a note that was never its own, after which A served the untracked mount it had suppressed. |
| P1-2: `None == None` was an identity match | `Claimant::is` compared `Option<String>` digests directly, so two artifacts whose manifests carry NO digest - anything sealed before the field existed - were "the same artifact" to every wall built on identity: `mount_for_lease`, reconciliation, unmount ownership, and the serving gate. Arbitrary content could be swapped under one `origin@version` and pass all four. The identity work of P.4c and P.4d rested on a comparison that treated ignorance as agreement. |
| Change | The orphan set is a plain `OrphanedMounts` owned by the memory actor and threaded through `lease_pack` and `enforce_lease_visibility`; the gate now `retain`s it against the engine's own mounts, so a note is dropped when its pack really goes and one mind can neither suppress nor clear another's. Identity requires a real digest: `Claimant::is` matches only when BOTH sides carry a non-blank digest that is equal, and the signer agrees; absence and blankness are refusals. `leasable_identity` refuses the GRANT outright for a pack with no verifiable digest, rather than leaving it to be mis-identified later - such a pack can still be mounted or installed by hand, it simply cannot be borrowed. |
| Tests | Two minds in one process: A is driven into an untracked mount (grant tx + unmount + quarantine all failing) and must not serve it; B then leases the SAME pack legitimately and its recall and prompt block work normally; and after B's turns, A is still suppressed - neither direction leaks. Identity: two legacy artifacts with digest and signer absent do not match each other or themselves, a blank digest is absence wearing a string, a real digest identifies across a changed path, a different digest does not, a re-signature is a different grant, and `leasable_identity` refuses None/""/"  " while passing a real one. Also strengthened the orphan test: after a manual detach the note is dropped, and a FRESH mount of the same pack is not suppressed by a stale note. |
| Actual metric | mind-memory 78/0 (+2), workspace 1023/0 (42 crates). |
| Not claimed | That legacy packs are supported by the lease line: they are explicitly refused, which is a product decision made here and recorded rather than discovered later. A host-side digest fallback for such artifacts is possible and is NOT built - refusing is honest, deriving a digest the publisher never signed would be inventing identity. |
| Not tested end-to-end | A pack file whose manifest genuinely lacks a digest could not be synthesised: the current engine always writes one at seal. The rule is therefore proven at the decision level (`Claimant::is` and `leasable_identity` as pure functions over the exact shapes) rather than through a sealed legacy artifact, and this row says so rather than implying broader coverage. |
| Decision | **KEEP - the lease-state and identity side PASSED** Codex's review of 8990fbb. The recorder side ran separately through E.PK4f and E.PK4g. |

## E.PK4f - the outbox's promise, made true (from Codex's independent recorder review)

*Amends E.PK4c's outbox work. Five findings. The promise "acknowledge only what is durable" was written in P.4c and was not true: four separate paths could acknowledge an event the log did not honestly contain.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| P1-a: check and append were not one act | `record_once` released the id cache before appending, so two concurrent drains could both look, both miss, and both write. A lock inside one `DecisionLog` would not have helped either: two handles can address the same file. |
| P1-b: a torn tail was appended through | A crash mid-write leaves a partial line. `read_events` skips what it cannot parse and `chain_head` falls back to genesis, so the next append concatenated its event onto the fragment, returned `Written` after a successful sync, and the drain acknowledged an event the chain could no longer verify. |
| P1-c: an ambiguous write was assumed, not resolved | `sync_all` can fail after the bytes have landed. The cache was neither updated nor invalidated, so a retry could write the event a second time. |
| P1-d: `Disabled` was acknowledged | `RecordOutcome::Disabled.is_durable()` is false, and the drain acknowledged it anyway "so the outbox would not grow without end". `ConversationEngine::new` leaves the recorder disabled, so any host that forgot `with_recorder` deleted its own lease evidence one event at a time. Convenience for eval harnesses had been allowed to outrank the outbox's entire purpose. |
| P1-e: the dedupe read an unverified log | The id set came from `read_events`, which parses without checking the chain. A forged line carrying a real event id would answer "already present", and the outbox would acknowledge the real event on the strength of the forgery. |
| Change | The whole check-scan-append-remember sequence runs under a lock keyed by the log's CANONICAL PATH (`PATH_LOCKS`), so every handle to one file serialises. `read_events_verified` walks the chain from genesis, returns the first bad line index, and treats a trailing partial line as corruption; `record_once` reads ids only through it, refuses to append when the log does not verify, and never writes through corruption. On an append error the head and id caches are dropped and the log is re-verified IN THE SAME critical section: durable only if the valid chain really contains the id, `Failed` otherwise. `Disabled` is never acknowledged - the events stay in the outbox and the drain says how many are waiting, naming the missing decision log. |
| Tests | A torn tail: a fragment is appended by hand, the next `record_once` fails with "does not verify", and nothing is written through it. A forged line: `read_events` is fooled (asserted, because that is the point), `read_events_verified` is not, and `record_once` refuses. Concurrency: eight threads, eight SEPARATE handles, one file, one id - exactly one `Written`, seven `AlreadyPresent`, and the chain still verifies with one event in it. Disabled: a mind built without `with_recorder` leases a pack, the event is kept, the drain names the backlog and the missing log, and the event is still there afterwards. |
| Actual metric | mind-observability 14/0 (+1), mind-conversation 426/0 (+1), workspace 1024/0 (42 crates). |
| P3, and a correction to E.PK4c's own record | Codex found a stray `#[test]` my P.4c patch had left stacked on the new recorder test - which is why that test appeared to run twice - and `appends_reads_and_verifies` silently lost its own attribute and stopped running at all. E.PK4c's "Found while writing the tests" row blamed "this crate's tests compile into more than one binary". **That explanation was wrong**, and it is corrected here rather than quietly edited there: the cause was a defect my own patch introduced, and I papered over its symptom with a unique path instead of finding it. The vacuous final re-drain in the integration test is replaced by a real replay through `record_once`. A pre-existing instance of the identical defect in `mind-inference` (a test that had been running twice since `61c864c`) is fixed in the same commit - the workspace count drops by one for that reason, which is the count getting more honest rather than a test being lost. |
| Not claimed | Cross-process durability: two OS processes appending to one log would still race, the lock is process-scoped, and a file lock is not built. This mind runs one process; the limit is stated rather than papered over. |
| Decision | KEEP - its five findings are closed, and the race that survived inside the fix is closed in E.PK4g, which PASSED. |

## E.PK4g - the lock was shared and the memory was not (from Codex's review of e814ea3)

*Amends E.PK4f. Two P1s and one P2. P.4f made the sequence atomic and left every handle its own idea of what the file contained, which is the same bug wearing a lock.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| P1-1: a shared lock over private memory | `PATH_LOCKS` serialised writers, but `seen_ids` and `head` stayed PER HANDLE and survived another handle's append. The interleaving Codex spelled out: A records (its caches go warm), B records `x` under the same lock, A records `x` - takes the lock, trusts its stale id set, does not find `x`, and appends it a second time onto a head that B had already superseded. Duplicate event, broken chain, both under a lock that was working exactly as designed. |
| Why my own test could not see it | P.4f's concurrency test spawned a FRESH `DecisionLog` per thread. Every writer therefore scanned the file after taking the lock, which is precisely the case that has no bug. The test proved the lock worked and could not, by construction, say anything about warm handles - it tested the mechanism I had built rather than the property I had promised. |
| P1-2: `record` wrote without the lock | Only `record_once` took it. An ordinary decision event could therefore interleave with a durable delivery and leave both chaining onto a head the other had superseded. |
| P2: two locks for one file | `path_lock` keyed on `canonicalize`, which FAILS on a path that does not exist yet - so the handle that created the log keyed on the relative path and every handle afterwards keyed on the canonical one. No mutual exclusion across the file's own creation. |
| Change | The handle now holds NO chain state at all: the head lives in `PathState` beside the file's lock, shared by every handle that names the file. `record` takes the same lock and the same head discipline as `record_once`. `record_once` performs a STRICT VERIFIED RESCAN every time and derives the head from that scan - nothing remembered is trusted, which for a low-volume outbox costs one small read and removes a whole class of defect. `lock_key` canonicalises the PARENT and joins the file name, so a key is absolute and stable whether or not the file exists yet. `append_chained` takes the previous head explicitly and returns the new one; the old `append_inner`, which read a per-handle head, is gone. |
| Tests | The exact interleaving: A(seed) warms a handle, B(x) writes, A(x) must answer AlreadyPresent and the chain must verify at length 2 - then a plain `record` from B, and A's next durable write must chain onto it. Mixed contention: six threads plus one warm handle interleaving `record` and `record_once`, asserting ONE unbroken chain over every line and no event id appearing twice. Key stability: a handle made before the file exists and one made after must key identically, the key must be absolute, and a relative spelling of the same file must key the same way. |
| Actual metric | mind-observability 17/0 (+3), workspace 1027/0 (42 crates). |
| The pattern, third instance | E.PK4d recorded that each fix in this line created the next defect. This is the same shape one level down: the fix for a race introduced a subtler race, and the test I wrote for it was shaped by the mechanism rather than by the promise. A test written by the author of a fix tends to exercise the code path the author was thinking about - which is exactly the path that is already correct. |
| Not claimed | Cross-process durability, still: the lock is process-scoped and two OS processes appending to one log would race. Unchanged from E.PK4f and stated again rather than allowed to fade. |
| Decision | **KEEP - PASSED.** Codex reviewed the isolated diff and implementation and closed the P.4 lifecycle + durable-outbox gate on 9959593, combined with the approved lease side of E.PK4e. |

## E.18 - pin the PUBLISHED yantrikdb 0.18.0 instead of a local checkout - pre-registered before the dependency is touched

*Pre-registration. Written and committed before a single line of `Cargo.toml` changes. This slice moves the substrate under every crate in the workspace, so it gets an entry rather than a version bump.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Why now | Core published 0.18.0 to crates.io and PyPI on 2026-08-26, fresh-install verified, all 40 published packs mounting. Two things follow. The mind currently builds against a PATH dependency into a working checkout that sits at 0.16.0 with uncommitted changes in it - a build nobody else could reproduce and that changes under us whenever that tree changes. And 0.16 carries the pack-mount regression (`no such column: synthesis_state`) that made E.PK4's attach-harm wall unrunnable in this repo's own harness. |
| Hypothesis under test | The workspace builds and passes green against the published 0.18.0 with NO source changes beyond the dependency line - i.e. nothing this mind depends on changed incompatibly between 0.16 and 0.18. |
| What is expected to CHANGE, measurably | The gated attach-harm control (`YM_PACK_DIST`, E.PK4 wall 1) becomes runnable in-process for the first time: 0.16 could not mount any 0.15-sealed pack, and 0.18 mounts all 40. Its result on the real artifacts is the number this slice exists to obtain locally, and the box's 0.15 run stays as separate evidence rather than being replaced by it. |
| Expected metric | Workspace green at its current count (1027/0 across 42 crates) or higher, and the gated attach-harm control reporting ZERO rows cleared across the twelve no-pack queries, matching what the box reported at 0.15. |
| KILL criteria, fixed now | (1) If the workspace does not build against published 0.18.0 without touching non-dependency source, the pin is REVERTED and the incompatibility reported to Core rather than worked around here. (2) If any currently-green test goes red, the pin is REVERTED - a substrate upgrade may not be paid for with a weakened assertion. (3) If the attach-harm control clears ANY row for a no-pack query, that is KILL for the affected pack's floor, exactly as pre-registered in E.PK4, and it is reported as an attach-harm finding rather than as a dependency problem. |
| Not claimed | That 0.18 improves anything for this mind. Its published `pack_context_for` / `recall_from_packs_for` are NOT used by this slice; wiring them is P.4b, which stays blocked on EVIDENCE (E.PK3c's 2/4), not on the engine. A dependency upgrade earns no behaviour change. |
| Reachability (Doctrine 1) | Not satisfied by a green build. The slice is not done until the box runs the pinned build on the production path and a real turn is served from it. |
| ACTUAL RESULT - the hypothesis is FALSIFIED | The published crate fetched and built cleanly, and then `mind-memory` failed to compile: `YantrikDB::recall` takes 16 arguments in 0.18.0 and 14 in 0.16.0. ONE call site, ONE error. The two new parameters are `event_after` / `event_before` (issue #149 phase 2), valid-time bounds whose documented behaviour with `None` is exactly the 0.16 behaviour - an additive, behaviour-preserving extension. So "nothing changed incompatibly" is false as stated, and the change is benign as measured. |
| Decision | **KILL, as pre-registered.** Criterion (1) fires: the workspace does not build against 0.18.0 without touching non-dependency source. The pin is REVERTED and the workspace is back on the path dependency, green. |
| Why it was reverted rather than argued away | Two `None`s at one call site is obviously harmless, and that is exactly why the criterion had to be honoured. A pre-registered kill criterion that is reinterpreted in the author's favour AFTER the result is known is not a criterion, it is a decoration - and this ledger's most useful entries (E.P2, E.D2) are the ones where the discipline caught its own author. The cost of honouring it is one extra commit; the cost of not honouring it is that no future criterion means anything. |
| What it bought | The falsification is worth more than the pin would have been: the upgrade is now known to be a one-line source change with named semantics, rather than assumed to be free. E.18b carries that knowledge as a claim that can be checked BEFORE the change, which is what the first entry could not do. |

## E.18b - pin published yantrikdb 0.18.0, adapting the ONE call site whose signature grew - pre-registered

*Pre-registration, written after E.18 was killed and before the change is made again. Everything E.18 could only hypothesise is now stated as a checkable claim.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| The change, stated exactly | `Cargo.toml`: `yantrikdb-core = { version = "0.18", package = "yantrikdb" }` in place of the path dependency. `crates/mind-memory/src/lib.rs:1917`: the single `db.recall(...)` call gains two trailing arguments, `None, None`. Nothing else in any crate changes. |
| The semantics of the two new arguments, claimed BEFORE the change | `event_after: Option<f64>` and `event_before: Option<f64>` are valid-time bounds in epoch seconds (yantrikdb issue #149 phase 2). When either is set they define the ELIGIBLE UNIVERSE before relevance ranking and top_k (filter-first), rows with a NULL `event_time_min` are excluded, and `event_after > event_before` is a typed `InvalidInput`. With BOTH `None` - which is what this slice passes - no temporal filter applies and the call behaves exactly as it does at 0.16. This mind does not use valid-time filtering anywhere, so passing `None` is the whole of the adaptation and not a deferral. |
| Expected metric | Workspace green at 1027/0 or higher across 42 crates, with no test weakened or removed. |
| What is expected to become possible | The gated attach-harm control (`YM_PACK_DIST`, E.PK4 wall 1) runs in this repo's own harness for the first time - 0.16 could mount no 0.15-sealed pack, 0.18 mounts all 40. |
| KILL criteria | (1) Any test that is green before the pin going red after it - a substrate upgrade may not be paid for with a weakened assertion. (2) Any source change beyond the two described above being needed: that would mean the incompatibility is wider than measured, and it goes back to Core rather than being absorbed here.
