(3) The attach-harm control clearing ANY row on a no-pack query is KILL for that pack's floor per E.PK4, reported as an attach-harm finding rather than as a dependency problem. |
| Not claimed | That 0.18 improves anything for this mind. `pack_context_for` / `recall_from_packs_for` remain UNWIRED; that is P.4b and it stays blocked on evidence (E.PK3c), not on the engine. |
| Reachability (Doctrine 1) | A green build is not the slice. It is not done until the box builds the pinned tree and serves a real turn from it. |
| Actual metric | Workspace **1027/0 across 42 crates** - identical to the count before the pin, with nothing weakened, removed or ignored. The diff is exactly the two changes registered: 8 lines in `Cargo.toml` (7 of them the comment explaining the pin) and 7 in `mind-memory` (5 of them the comment explaining the two `None`s). No other crate needed anything. |
| THE WALL THAT COULD NOT RUN HERE, RUN HERE | The gated attach-harm control executed in this repo's own harness for the first time, and against MORE artifacts than the box could offer: **40 of 40 published packs mounted** - every version of every pack in `packs/dist`, including the whole `web-craft` 0.1/0.2/0.3.0/0.3.1 and `mcp-spec` 0.1 through 0.3.2 lineages that the box's library does not carry - and **ZERO rows cleared the 0.55 wall on all twelve no-pack queries**. The box's 13-pack run at 0.15 stands as separate evidence rather than being replaced: two engines, two pack sets, same answer. |
| Decision | **KEEP the adaptation, RETRACT the word "pin".** Both new arguments behaved exactly as claimed and the 1027/0 + 40/40 + zero-harm evidence stands for the artifact that was tested. But `version = "0.18"` is not a pin - Cargo reads it as caret `^0.18` - and this repo does not track `Cargo.lock`, so "the build is reproducible from published artifacts" was FALSE as written and the code comment saying `PINNED` was false with it. Corrected in E.18c. |

## E.18c - a caret is not a pin (from Codex's review of 2c51569)

*Amends E.18b. One release blocker, and it is a claim rather than a defect: the code did what it said only if you did not read Cargo's version grammar.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| What was wrong | `yantrikdb-core = { version = "0.18", package = "yantrikdb" }` is **caret `^0.18`** to Cargo: any future 0.18.x satisfies it. The comment above that line said `PINNED TO THE PUBLISHED CRATE` and E.18b's decision row said the build was "reproducible from published artifacts". Neither was true. A 0.18.1 published tomorrow would have changed the substrate under this mind silently, and there would have been nothing in the repository to notice it with - because `Cargo.lock` is in `.gitignore` (line 2) and `git ls-files Cargo.lock` returns nothing. The evidence for what actually resolved lived only in an ignored file on one machine. |
| Change | `version = "=0.18.0"` - the exact requirement Cargo needs to mean what the comment claimed. The comment is rewritten to say what it now does, and to name the limit it does NOT cover. |
| The resolved artifact, recorded here because the lockfile cannot | **yantrikdb 0.18.0**, `registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index`, checksum `8ff1ffe72dc393998df748020b12134eaaa1a1fb85d653c632f28c6fb06bbb42`. Independently quoted by Codex from their own resolution before I recorded it, which makes it a two-witness fact rather than a number I read off my own machine. |
| Re-run on the exact requirement | Workspace **1027/0** across 42 crates, unchanged. Focused pack wall: **40 of 40** published packs mounted, **zero rows cleared** on all twelve no-pack queries, unchanged. Same checksum resolved before and after, so the artifact under test never moved - the requirement did. |
| RESIDUAL, stated because it is the honest limit | `=0.18.0` pins the ENGINE exactly. **The rest of the dependency graph still floats**, because the lockfile remains untracked - a `serde` or `rusqlite` point release can still change this build without a commit. Tracking `Cargo.lock` is the standard answer for an application workspace and is NOT done in this slice: it is a repository-convention decision with its own consequences, and Codex offered it as an alternative rather than an addition. Recorded as owed, not as done. |
| Why this entry exists at all | The defect was in a claim, not in behaviour: the code worked, the tests passed, the deploy was healthy, and the sentence describing it was false. That is the most dangerous kind of entry in this ledger and the easiest to leave uncorrected, because nothing is broken. E.18b's decision row is amended in place rather than rewritten, so the overclaim and its correction both stay visible. |
| Live (box, 8072a6f DEPLOYED health-ok 2026-08-26T04:50:14Z) - Doctrine 1 satisfied | THE ARTIFACT, THIRD WITNESS: the box's own lockfile resolves `yantrikdb 0.18.0` with checksum `8ff1ffe7...bbb42` - the same value as this machine and as Codex's independent resolution, now reproduced on a THIRD machine that fetched it from crates.io itself. That is what "reproducible" was supposed to mean and could not, before the requirement was exact. A REAL TURN: "in one sentence: what is coyote time?" answered correctly in 42 s on the production path. AND THE PART THAT MATTERS MOST for this slice - `ym pack probe` on the pinned engine returns **byte-identical similarities to the 0.16 runs**: web-craft's popover row at **0.566**, container queries at **0.476**, empty-states at **0.439**, one row cleared and fourteen withheld by the floor. The single call site this slice changed is the pack-recall path, and it produces the same numbers on a different engine - which is the strongest available evidence that `event_after: None, event_before: None` is the no-op the pre-registration claimed it was, rather than an assumption that happened not to blow up. |
| Decision | **KEEP.** |

## E.PK3d - the live router split, grown from the recorder instead of by hand - pre-registered

*Pre-registration, written before the extractor exists. This entry builds the INSTRUMENT and the PROTOCOL for a future router revision; it does not revise the router, and it cannot: `coverage-router-v1` stays SHADOWED at floor 0.50 / margin 0.05 throughout, unchanged.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| The claim being made honest | E.PK3c says the live split "accumulates from the recorder by itself". It does not. Its four cases were read off a `pack_route_shadow` dump and typed into `live_cases()` by hand, which is fine for four and is not a mechanism. A split that grows only when someone remembers to copy rows will stop growing, and the sentence claiming otherwise will quietly become false - the same shape as E.18b's "pinned" comment over a caret range. |
| What this slice builds | An extractor in `mind-evals` that reads a decision log and emits the raw material of the split: every `pack_route_shadow` goal, verbatim, with the ranking and verdict the router recorded beside it, deduplicated by query text, and carrying NO label. Labels are never derived from the routes - that is the whole point of the split - so what it emits is deliberately unusable until an independent witness has answered. |
| The labelling protocol, fixed now | Queries go to a witness who has NOT seen the rankings or verdicts, with the pack coverage descriptions and one instruction: answer with a pack id, or NONE. This is how E.PK3c's four were labelled (Codex, blind) and it stays the rule. My own labels are never admissible: I have seen the routes, and labelling after seeing them is the bias the frozen corpus exists to prevent (Doctrine 3). |
| Strata the split must reach before it can carry a bar (Codex's requirement) | (1) PROJECT-NAME TOKEN COLLISIONS - an entity whose NAME contains a pack's subject word, e.g. `saga-mcp` pulling `mcp-spec`, which is the false lease E.PK3c already caught. (2) POLYSEMY - a word that means different things in two packs' vocabularies, e.g. "buffering" between `input buffering` and `buffer overflows`, which is the miss E.PK3c already caught. (3) HARD NEGATIVES - queries no pack covers, labelled NONE explicitly rather than by absence. The seed of four covers one instance of each of (1) and (2) and one NONE; a bar needs many more, and the split is not scorable against one until it has them. |
| TWO metrics, never one number (Codex's requirement) | ROUTE ACCURACY = leases matching the witness's label / queries the witness labelled with a pack. ATTACH HARM = leases granted on queries the witness labelled NONE / queries labelled NONE. They are reported separately and never averaged: a router that leases nothing scores perfectly on the second and uselessly on the first, and one number would hide which. |
| What would earn a router revision (NOT part of this slice) | A pre-registered bar on a split large enough to carry one, with the strata above represented, labelled blind. This entry deliberately does not name that bar: naming it now, from a four-case sample I have already seen, would be fitting the target to the data. The bar is written when the split is big enough that it cannot be. |
| KILL criteria | (1) The extractor inventing, editing or normalising a query - it copies verbatim or it is wrong. (2) Any label being derivable from the recorded route inside this repo: if a label can be computed, it is not a witness's answer. (3) Any change to `coverage-router-v1`'s floor, margin or policy id in this slice. |
| Not claimed | That a bigger split will vindicate the router. E.PK3c's 2/4 may well hold up, and the honest outcome of a larger split may be that `coverage-router-v1` is retired rather than tuned. The instrument is being built to find out, not to win. |
| Built | `extract_live_routes` in `mind-evals`, reading through the VERIFIED chain (an unverified log errors rather than being scraped), deduplicating by exact query text, counting repeats as demand, and flagging when one query's top pack MOVES between routings. `ExtractedRoute` has nowhere to put a label - kill criterion (2) is structural rather than promised. `render_for_witness` emits the numbered queries and nothing else; `render_cases` emits `LiveCase` rows carrying the ranking as evidence with `label: None`. |
| THREE THINGS THE TESTS FOUND, none of which I expected | (1) The recorder TRIMS and TRUNCATES: `DecisionEvent::sanitized` applies `brief(goal, 160)` on append, so a long question reaches the corpus shortened to 160 characters plus an ellipsis while the router routed all of it live. A case built from it would be labelled on less than the mind was asked. Pinned by a test, and recorded as the split's inherited fidelity limit. (2) A goal the recorder refused to hold arrives as the literal `[redacted-secret]`, which is not a question anyone asked and can never be labelled - dropped rather than carried. (3) **THE ONE THAT MATTERED.** I wrote a test asserting a card-shaped number would be redacted. It is not: `mind_types::contains_secret` matches a short list of TOKEN PREFIXES (`ghp_`, `glpat-`, `akia`, `xoxb-`, `sk-`, PEM headers, `app password`) and NOT the generic "api key" / "password" this row first claimed - Codex corrected that, and the true inventory is in E.SEC1. A bare run of digits carries no marker, so `my card pin is 4471-9302-1122-8890 what coyote time` is written to the log verbatim. This protocol's entire purpose is to send real user queries to an OUTSIDE agent, so that would have gone out with the next labelling request. |
| The gate that finding earned | `looks_sensitive`, applied in the extractor before anything can leave the building: `contains_secret`, plus any digit run of 12 or more ignoring the separators people put inside numbers. It errs toward withholding - a false positive costs one corpus row, a false negative costs something that cannot be undone - and the withheld COUNT is carried in `LiveCorpus` and stated in the witness request, so a corpus that shrinks says so. |
| Reported, not fixed here | `contains_secret` not matching numeric shapes is `mind-types`' business and it guards MEMORY WRITES as well as this log. Widening it would change what the mind is willing to remember, which is a decision with its own consequences and does not belong folded into an eval slice. Recorded for Core and for whoever takes that decision. |
| Actual metric | mind-evals 28/0 (+2), workspace 1029/0 (42 crates). |
| Not claimed | That the split is now big enough to score anything. It has four labelled cases and a mechanism; the bar stays unwritten until the split is large enough that naming it cannot be fitting the target to the data. |
| Decision | **KEEP.** The instrument exists, the protocol is enforced by tests rather than by intention, and the split can now grow without anyone remembering to copy rows. |

## E.19 - track Cargo.lock: an application should ship the resolution it was tested against - pre-registered

*Pre-registration, written before `.gitignore` is touched. Sequenced by Codex ahead of the live-split corpus design, and scoped by them: this slice changes NO source and NO dependency requirement. It writes down a resolution that already exists.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| The residual this closes | E.18c pinned the ENGINE exactly (`=0.18.0`) and said plainly that the rest of the graph still floats, because `Cargo.lock` is in `.gitignore` (line 2). A `serde` or `rusqlite` point release can therefore still change this build with no commit anywhere, and the only evidence of what actually resolved lives in an ignored file on one machine. For a library that is correct; for an APPLICATION it is a gap, and this workspace builds a binary that runs unattended on a box. |
| The change, exactly and only | (1) Remove `Cargo.lock` - and only that line - from `.gitignore`. (2) `git add Cargo.lock` as it already stands. Nothing else: no `Cargo.toml` edit, no version requirement touched, no source file, no `cargo update`. If adding the lockfile as it stands requires the resolver to change anything, that is a KILL rather than a thing to accept. |
| Recorded in this entry, because a lockfile is evidence only if someone can check it | The SHA-256 of the committed `Cargo.lock`, and `yantrikdb 0.18.0` checksum `8ff1ffe72dc393998df748020b12134eaaa1a1fb85d653c632f28c6fb06bbb42` - the value already witnessed three times (this machine, Codex's own resolution, the box's independent fetch). |
| Gates (Codex's, adopted verbatim) | `cargo metadata --locked` succeeds; the full workspace passes under `--locked` at its current count of **1029/0** across 42 crates (1027 in Codex's message, +2 from E.PK3d's tests since); the focused P.4 and pack-wall runs are unchanged; the BOX builds clean from a fresh reset with the lockfile in place and does not require an unlocked update; and a real turn is served afterwards. |
| KILL criteria | (1) Any resolver or source change not already present - i.e. `--locked` failing, or the act of committing the lock altering a single resolved version. (2) Any test regression. (3) The box needing to update the lock to build. Any of these and `.gitignore` goes back as it was. |
| RESIDUAL, stated now rather than discovered later (Codex's wording, adopted) | A lockfile freezes REGISTRY and GIT resolution. It does **not** freeze the local `path` dependencies into `../yantrik-companion` (`yantrik-ml`, `yantrik-os`, `yantrik-chat`) or the `[patch]` redirect: those carry no checksum, are whatever that working tree contains at build time, and are NOT made reproducible by this slice. The mind's engine is pinned and verified; three sibling crates remain mutable, and no amount of lockfile makes them otherwise. |
| Not claimed | That the build becomes reproducible. It becomes reproducible IN ITS REGISTRY GRAPH, which is a real and checkable improvement and is not the same sentence - the mistake E.18b made by writing "reproducible" over a caret range. |
| Actual metric | `cargo metadata --locked` succeeded BEFORE staging, so committing the lock altered no resolved version - kill criterion (1) checked rather than assumed. Full workspace under `--locked`: **1029/0** across 42 crates. Pack wall under `--locked`: 40/40 mounted, zero rows cleared on all twelve no-pack queries. Committed lock: sha256 `c8fb17f2ac95d3dfb5489f4a595c173276f5b51b9165c9971a436dc9c6d7bc8e`, 110,656 bytes. |
| Live (box, f2df384 DEPLOYED health-ok 2026-08-26T05:08:58Z) | After a fresh `reset --hard origin/main` the box's `Cargo.lock` hashes to **the same `c8fb17f2ac...`** as this machine - two machines, one resolution, verified by hash rather than asserted - and `cargo metadata --locked` succeeds there, so kill criterion (3) does not fire. The release build then completed and `git status --short Cargo.lock` came back EMPTY: the build needed no lock change, which is the property the whole slice is for. A real turn answered correctly in 61 s, and `ym pack probe` still returns the same 0.566 / 14-withheld shape as every run since E.18b. |
| Decision | **KEEP.** |

## E.SEC1 - one typed sensitivity detector, wired to every boundary that leaks - pre-registered

*Pre-registration only; the implementation is a separate commit at Codex's instruction. Sequenced by them AHEAD of the recorder-grown corpus: until this passes and its audit is clean, no real query corpus leaves the box.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| THE TRUE BASELINE, inventoried rather than assumed | I claimed `contains_secret` "finds api key, password and their neighbours". It does not, and Codex caught the claim. The whole list is fifteen substrings: `ghp_ gho_ ghu_ ghs_ github_pat_ glpat- akia asia -----begin "private key" "app password" app-password xoxb- xoxp- sk-`. That is a TOKEN-PREFIX detector with two PEM markers and one mail-app phrase. There is no generic `password`, no `api key`, no `bearer`, no `secret key`, no `access token`, no `client secret`, no `passcode`. |
| It fails in BOTH directions, and the second is worse | MISSES, verified: `my password is hunter2`, `the api key is abc123xyz`, `bearer eyJ...`, `client secret: ...`, `ssn 123-45-6789`, `access token = ...`, and the card PIN that opened this. FALSE POSITIVES, verified: `sk-` matches inside **task-**, **risk-**, **desk-**, **ask-**; `asia` matches inside **Asia**, **Malaysia**, **asian**. And `gate_write` REFUSES on a hit, so this mind currently cannot remember "asian food recipes" or "remind me about the task-list" while it will happily remember a password. A detector that refuses ordinary life and admits credentials is not conservative in either direction. |
| The change (Codex's design, adopted) | A typed, high-precision detector in `mind-types` returning `SensitiveFinding` KINDS rather than a bare bool, with `contains_secret` retained as a compatibility wrapper so no existing caller changes meaning by accident. Required detections: the existing token prefixes and PEM; credential PHRASES paired with a value (`password`, `passcode`, `api key`, `secret key`, `access token`, `client secret`, `bearer`); payment PANs as 13-19 digits passing LUHN even with no surrounding context; card/PIN/CVV context paired with a numeric candidate, so the reported `4471-9302-1122-8890` is caught even though it fails Luhn; SSN-like patterns WITH context. |
| The negative fixtures are part of the contract, not an afterthought | Benign order and tracking IDs, timestamps, phone-like text, UUIDs, IP addresses, version strings, and `80-100 ms` must all pass clean - as must `asian`, `task-list`, `risk-register`, `desk-space`, `ask-me`. Blanket rejection of every long number is explicitly NOT the fix; it is the current failure mode wearing a new coat. |
| Four boundaries, ONE finding | The same typed finding drives: memory-write refusal, observability FULL-FIELD redaction, egress denial, and eval withholding. End-to-end tests at each boundary must prove the raw value never appears in the output OR in the error text - a refusal that quotes what it refused is the leak it was meant to prevent. |
| Then, and only then: a READ-ONLY audit | Historical memory and decision logs on the box, reporting COUNTS, KINDS and record ids or file names - never a matching value, not even truncated. The decision logs are hash-chained, so nothing is rewritten ad hoc: any remediation is proposed first as quarantine / rotation / redacted migration, with a backup and chain-preservation evidence, and is its own entry. |
| KILL criteria | (1) Any negative fixture regressing - a detector that refuses ordinary text is the bug being fixed, not the fix. (2) Any raw sensitive value appearing in a log line, an error message or a test failure message. (3) Any boundary left wired to the old bool while another uses the typed finding: one finding or it does not ship. (4) Rewriting a hash-chained log during the audit. |
| Not claimed | That a detector can be complete. It will miss things, and the design admits that by keeping the four boundaries independent - the point is that one improvement lands at all four at once, not that the list is finished. |
| Gate this holds open | E.PK3d's mechanism stays committed as evidence, and no recorder-grown corpus is extracted from or exported off the box until this entry passes with a clean audit. |
| Implementation | `fbd5d2d`, separate from the pre-registration at `9f4e2bf` as instructed. Workspace **1035/0** under `--locked` across 42 crates. |
| Thresholds were calibrated by tests I did not write | Two PRE-EXISTING tests carried short stand-ins - `ghp_SECRET12345` and `sk-abc123` - and my first token-tail thresholds silently stopped catching both. They were right and I was wrong: what keeps false positives out is the TOKEN BOUNDARY, not the length, so a truncated paste is still a credential. Tails now sit well below the real formats. |
| Improved beyond the plan | The recorder now redacts the card query at WRITE time, so it never reaches the log for the eval gate to withhold later. E.PK3d's test asserts that stronger property rather than the weaker one it had assumed. |
| THE AUDIT FOUND A BUG IN THE DETECTOR, which is what a second implementation is for | The audit is a deliberate re-implementation in Python, so that agreement is evidence rather than a shared assumption. Its first field-aware pass flagged **28 payment-card hits in `mind.db.read_receipts.jsonl`**. Reporting those as leaks would have been wrong: every one sat in the `chain` field - SHA-256 hash-chain values - located WITHOUT printing any value, by reporting the JSON key path and digit-run length only. A 64-character hex hash contains a card-shaped substring often enough to matter (28 of 11,866 lines ≈ 0.24%). A real card stands as its own token or is grouped with spaces and hyphens; it is never buried in hex. `is_embedded` now excludes any digit run with a letter pressed against either end, in the detector AND in the audit, with the two real chain hashes from this project as fixtures. |
| AUDIT RESULT (read-only, box, after the fix) | `mind.db.decisions.jsonl` 154 events, **0 flagged**. `egress_receipts.jsonl` 629 lines, **0**. `mind.db.read_receipts.jsonl` 11,868 lines, **0**. `tape.jsonl` 411 lines, **0**. `mind.db` 78 memories, **2 flagged `credential-phrase`**, rids `01a00bd3-ed38-7322-979b-cc7bc1ea491f` and `01a00bd9-dd71-72c8-be8e-607d770508c7`. Counts, kinds and record ids only; no value was printed, and nothing was written to anything that was read. |
| What the two flagged memories are NOT | Confirmed leaks. `credential-phrase` fires on a credential word with a plausible value beside it, which also describes a note ABOUT a credential. I have not read them, because reading them to check would be the thing the audit exists to avoid doing casually. They need a human decision, and remediation is its own entry. |
| Remediation, deliberately NOT done here | The decision logs are hash-chained and the memories are live. Nothing has been rewritten. A proposal - quarantine, rotation or redacted migration, with a backup and chain-preservation evidence - is owed as a separate entry, per Codex's instruction. |
| Not claimed | That the detector is complete. It will miss things; the design keeps the four boundaries reading ONE finding so that an improvement lands at all four at once, which is a different promise from completeness. |
| Gate | Still closed: no recorder-grown corpus is extracted or exported until Codex reviews this audit and says GO. |
| Decision | **KEEP** the detector; the audit's two memory findings are referred, not resolved. |

## E.SURF1 - `posture_json`: the executive's current reading, served to the cockpit

*A surface, not an experiment - so this entry records the CONTRACT and the two rules that keep it honest, and claims no result. Assigned to this workspace by Pranab; the desktop client (commit 14e0089) already renders it and shows an honest "predates" state until the box advertises it.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| What ships | `posture_json` in `TYPED_VERBS` (the handshake a client gates on), dispatched beside `orders_json`, returning `{decisions[], receptivity}` in snake_case. `decisions[]` mirrors `mind_proactive::ExecutiveDecision` one-for-one; `posture` goes through `posture_name` for the uppercase the client reads. |
| Rule 1: NOTHING IS SYNTHESISED | The row comes from `arbitrate()` over the same `candidate_for_digest` the proactive loop builds, so the pane shows what the executive would actually say right now. A test arbitrates the same candidate independently and asserts the surface agrees with it - if this ever started composing rows for the reply, the two would drift and the test would say so. |
| Rule 2: WHAT IS NOT KNOWN IS NOT CLAIMED | Quiet hours are a function of the user's clock and time zone, and that organ lives in `mind-core` (`in_quiet_hours_now`) - which is why `ex4_shadow_decide` takes it as a parameter rather than computing it. The loop now reads it ONCE per tick and deposits it via `note_observed_quiet`, so the pane and the arbiter cannot show different nights. If the loop has not run in this process there is no reading, and `decisions` is EMPTY rather than arbitrated against a guessed `false`. `user_receptive` is `null` whenever the world model has no bin - `proactive_receptivity_ok` answers TRUE for "unknown", which is right for a gate and wrong for a report, so the underlying reading is used directly. `observed_at_ms` is additive and is what makes the other two trustworthy: null means nothing was observed. |
| The drift that was waiting | `WakeCondition`'s variants are TUPLES, so a serde derive with `rename_all = "snake_case"` emits `deadline_within` - not the `deadline_within_ms` the client parses. Nothing in `mind-proactive` derives `Serialize` at all (it is a model-free waist, deliberately), so the wire form is written by hand and pinned by its own test. |
| Tests | The contract field by field in the wire form the client parses; empty-decisions-and-null-observed before any reading; a real arbitrated decision after one, checked against an independent `arbitrate()` call; quiet-hours ON and OFF producing different readings; the three wake conditions' exact wire names; and a handshake assertion that EVERY entry in `TYPED_VERBS` is advertised - a surface a client gates on that is served but not advertised leaves the pane dark forever. |
| Actual metric | mind-conversation 429/0 (+2), workspace 1037/0 (42 crates). |
| Not claimed | That the pane will show anything interesting. The only candidate this mind constructs is `periodic_digest`, so `decisions` holds zero or one row, and its posture is usually MONITOR or IGNORE. The surface reports the executive that exists (BENCHMARKED, shadow-only, deciding nothing - see the AGI roadmap's baseline table), not a busier one. |
| Live (box, f80cf8c health-ok 2026-08-26T05:47:22Z) | The handshake advertises `posture_json`; the surface answers. Before the loop ticked: `decisions: []`, `observed_at_ms: null` - the honest empty state. Within ~15 s the loop deposited a reading and the executive arbitrated a REAL decision: posture `MONITOR`, reason_code `quiet_hours`, `wake_when: [{state_change_of: receptivity}]`, `user_receptive: true`, `quiet_hours: true`. |
| AND IT IMMEDIATELY EARNED ITS KEEP - a units bug, live since EX4-LIVE-A | The live reply carried `quiet_hours_end_ms: 22380000` — about 6.2 HOURS, a duration, in a field the client reads as an epoch instant. `quiet_hours_end_in_ms()` returned "milliseconds UNTIL quiet hours end", and `arbitrate` copies that field straight into `MonitorPlan.review_at_ms`, whose sibling construction is `Some(cm.due_at_ms - DAY_MS)` — plainly an instant. So every quiet-hours decision EX4-LIVE-A has recorded carries a review time a few hours after the 1970 epoch. Nothing acted on it, because the executive is shadow-only, and nothing RENDERED it until this surface did — which is exactly why an unread field stays wrong. Fixed at the producer (`quiet_hours_end_at_ms`, name and arithmetic now agree), stated at the waist (the field had NO documented unit, which is why the guess was available to make), and asserted at the surface, where a wrong unit would be seen: anything below the year 2000 is a duration wearing a timestamp's name. |
| Decision | **KEEP.** |

## E.SK1 - an instruction document is a runnable skill, not a dead note - pre-registered

*Pre-registration, written before the dispatch is touched. The design is Pranab's, mapped to the exact call sites; every one of them was verified against the code before this entry was written.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| What is broken | `run_skill` (lib.rs:7762) parses a skill's `code` as a JSON capability spec and, when there is no `tool` key, returns `(skill 'x' has no runnable recipe spec yet)`. EVERY imported instruction document lands there - `import_agent` saves the markdown itself as `code` - so a document the mind was given, banked and can name is a note it will not execute, and the model improvises instead. |
| The recipe already exists | `import_skill.rs::import_agent` builds the right shape for SCHEDULED runs: `Think { "Follow these standing instructions exactly and produce the deliverable they describe:

{instructions}" , store_as: "result" }` then `Notify { "📥 [name] {{result}}" }`. On-call execution needs the same steps with the run's input woven in, so the construction is factored into ONE helper both callers use - two copies would drift, and the scheduled path is the one nobody watches. |
| A SECOND BUG, found while reading | The existing tool path calls `record_skill_outcome(&sk.name, true)` BEFORE `recipes.run_with(...)`. Every invocation is credited as a success before the work runs, so a skill that fails every time still reads as perfectly reliable. That is the same shape as E.PK2b - crediting an outcome that was never observed - and it corrupts the ledger that skill selection reads. Moved after the run, and reporting what actually happened. |
| Shape of the change | In the no-`tool` branch: build the instruction recipe with `target` woven in as `Input for this run: {target}`; take a job-board slot (`try_acquire_bg`); write a `running` row to the delegation ledger (`{id, name, task, kind: "skill", status, started_ms}`) so it appears on the cockpit board; `tokio::spawn` the run; `scratch_note` the flight trace first, then progress; on completion `record_skill_outcome` with the REAL result, `ledger_update` to `done`/`failed`, push the notification and release the slot. The tool call returns a RECEIPT immediately - a multi-minute research document must not block the turn that asked for it. |
| KILL criteria | (1) A tool call blocking on a document run. (2) Any outcome recorded before the work finishes, on either path. (3) The two callers of the instruction recipe diverging - one helper or it does not ship. (4) A ledger row for a run that cannot start (the executor-presence rule `delegate_cmd` already keeps: a row for a job that cannot run is a lie on the board). |
| Not claimed | That the documents will produce good work. This makes a banked instruction RUNNABLE and visible on the board; whether the deliverable is any good is the skill ledger's business, and it can only start measuring that once outcomes are recorded honestly. |
| Optional companion, deliberately out of scope for now | `delegate_cmd` matching a delegation name against a banked skill, so `delegate test-market: check WMT` runs the document. Same helper, one more caller - taken only after the dispatch path is green and reviewed. |
| Built | `import_skill::instruction_steps(name, instructions, input)` - ONE construction, used by `import_agent` for the standing schedule and by the new `run_instruction_skill` for an on-call run. The latter lives in `delegate.rs` because everything a document run needs IS the delegation machinery: the job-board slot, the ledger row the cockpit renders, the scratch notes that make a long run watchable, the notify queue. A document run is a delegation whose instructions were banked earlier instead of typed now. |
| Both bugs closed | The no-`tool` branch runs the document instead of refusing it, in the background, returning a receipt. And `record_skill_outcome` now runs AFTER `run_with` on BOTH paths, reporting `out.ok` - it used to credit success before the work started, so a skill that failed every time read as perfectly reliable in the ledger skill selection consults. |
| A PRE-EXISTING FLAKE, found because my tests moved the pid | `surface.rs`'s orders test opened `ym_surface_orders_{pid}.db` and never removed it; **188 of those files had accumulated in temp**. A recycled pid made the test read a previous run's two sleeping orders alongside its own and count four. It had passed for as long as it existed and failed the moment two unrelated tests shifted which pid it got - so the test was never right, only lucky. Now unique per run and removed through a `Drop` guard, so a firing assertion still cleans up. The 188 strays were deleted. |
| Actual metric | mind-conversation 431/0 (+2), workspace 1039/0 (42 crates). |
| Not claimed | That the documents produce good work. This makes a banked instruction RUNNABLE and visible on the board; whether a deliverable is any good is the skill ledger's business - and that ledger can only start meaning anything now that outcomes are recorded after the fact instead of before it. |
| Not done | The `delegate_cmd` companion (a delegation name matching a banked skill). Same helper, one more caller, deliberately left until this path is reviewed. |
| Decision | **KEEP.** |

## E.SK2 - the phrase a user actually types runs a THIRD executor, and it feeds prose to Python - pre-registered

*Found while trying to get Doctrine-1 live evidence for E.SK1: I asked the box to run a banked document and the run never reached the code I had fixed.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| How it surfaced | E.SK1 shipped and deployed (`9d8c53a health-ok`). Seeking live evidence, I sent a `/chat` turn asking the mind to run the banked document `test-market`. It never reached `run_skill`. A command shortcut intercepted the turn first. E.SK1's ledger row records TEST evidence only, and the reason is now clear: the path I fixed is not the path that runs. |
| The third executor | `skills.rs:408` handles the phrases `run skill X` / `use skill X` / `run the skill X` / `use the skill X` / `invoke skill X` and dispatches them ITSELF - `sb.run_python/run_shell/run_rust(&skill.code)` - with no reference to the tool arm. The language comes from `lang_from_str`, whose match is `"rust" | "shell" | _ => Python`. **Anything unrecognised is Python.** `import_agent` banks documents as `lang: "md"`; the capability importer banks `lang: "capability"`. Both are "anything unrecognised". So the phrase runs 6,149 characters of markdown prose through a Python interpreter, collects the SyntaxError, and records a failure. |
| The phrase is the one the mind advertises | This is not an obscure form. `suggest_skill` ends its own suggestion with `say "run skill {name}" to use it`, and the skill-search reply prints `→ "run skill {name}"` after every hit. The mind tells the user to type the sentence that cannot work. |
| LIVE evidence, already on the box, before any change | `mind_skills` on `192.168.4.90`: `deal-tracker-page` (prose, 478 b, `runs=4 ok=0`), `headroom-check` (prose, 675 b, `runs=4 ok=0`) - prose documents, four attempts each, **never once succeeded**. Against `csv-sum` (real Python, 59 b, `runs=4 ok=1`). The prose/code split predicts the success/failure split exactly. This is the failure already recorded in production, not a hypothesis. |
| A REGRESSION I SHIPPED IN E.SK1 | The tool arm classifies two ways: JSON with a `tool` key, or *everything else* → instruction document. **Real code is "everything else."** So `run_skill` on `csv-sum` now hands Python source to an LLM as "standing instructions to follow." Before E.SK1 that call was refused honestly; now it is confidently wrong, takes a job-board slot and records an outcome. E.SK1 removed a bad refusal and put a bad execution in its place - narrower than the bug it fixed, but mine, and shipped. |
| Root cause, named | Three call sites each decide independently what a skill IS: the tool arm by JSON shape, the phrase path by `lang` with a Python fallback, the scheduler not at all. E.SK1's own lesson was that two copies of a *recipe* drift; the same is true of the *classification*, and I did not apply it one level up. |
| Shape of the change | ONE `classify_skill(&Skill) -> SkillBody` with three arms - `Capability(tool)` (JSON carrying a `tool` key), `Code(CodeLang)` (`lang` is literally `python`/`shell`/`rust`), `Instructions` (everything else: `md`, `capability`, whatever a future importer writes). Both executors call it. `lang_from_str` is DELETED, not patched: it has exactly one caller and that caller is this bug. Unknown `lang` must mean prose, never Python - guessing an interpreter is what caused this. |
| Second change | `parse_run_skill` returns `(name, target)`, splitting on the first `:`, so `run skill test-market: check WMT` carries its input. Today the name is parsed as `test-market:` (the trailer strips quotes and dots, not colons) and the lookup fails outright. A document without its input is not worth running. |
| KILL criteria | (1) Any path that guesses an interpreter for an unrecognised `lang`. (2) Executable code reaching the instruction runner, or prose reaching the sandbox - either direction is a mis-execution and voids this. (3) The two executors classifying differently: one function or it does not ship. (4) A capability spec silently running as prose because it is easier than saying it needs a target. |
| Not claimed | That the documents produce good work - the same disclaimer E.SK1 carries. And NOT that this makes every phrasing work: the save-skill parser matches any sentence containing `save` and `skill`, which is how my first live attempt was swallowed. That is a real sharp edge, left alone here and noted rather than fixed in the same change. |
| **PRE-REGISTRATION CORRECTED** | The row above says the phrase path "collects the SyntaxError and records a failure". That is true only for raw markdown. The live probe falsified the general claim, and the truth is worse. I am correcting this here rather than editing the row, because the row was written before the probe and rewriting it would erase the fact that I predicted this wrongly (the E.PK4f precedent). |
| What the probe actually did | Two `/chat` turns on the box, before any change. `run skill csv-sum` (real Python) → `54 total`, exit 0 — the phrase path works, and the sandbox is real. `run skill headroom-check` → **`(no output)`, exit code 0**. Not an error. A clean, silent, successful-looking run. |
| The mechanism, corrected | `headroom-check`'s `code` is not prose: it is a JSON-encoded STRING wrapping a Python one-liner (`"import json, urllib.request, os; ..."`, quotes included). Fed to a Python interpreter, a bare string literal is VALID: it parses, evaluates, prints nothing, exits 0. And `ok = exit_code == 0 && !timed_out`, so **a no-op is recorded as a SUCCESS**. Three distinct fates, not one: raw markdown → SyntaxError → failure; a JSON object with `true`/`null` → NameError → failure; a JSON-encoded string → silent success for work never performed. |
| Why that is the serious half | A loud failure is a bug. A silent success is E.PK2b's shape again — an outcome credited for work nobody observed — and it was already in the ledger that skill selection consults, in production, arrived at by a different route than E.SK1's. Two independent paths reached the same corruption, which is what makes it a class and not an incident. |
| I CAUSED ONE | My single read-intent probe moved `headroom-check` from `runs=4 successes=0` to `runs=5 successes=1`. That false success is live on the box. I have NOT hand-corrected the counter: it is the evidence, a one-sample distortion on one skill, and quietly editing the ledger I am reporting on is exactly the move this entry exists to criticise. Flagged for Pranab's decision. |
| The banked corpus, measured not assumed | 10 skills. 3 well-formed specs (`web-monitor` 11/5, `github-monitor`, `inbox-monitor`); 3 double-encoded JSON strings mislabelled `lang=capability` — of which **two are prose** (`deal-tracker-page`, `photo_quality_filter`) and **one is Python** (`headroom-check`); 3 raw markdown documents (`test-market` 6,149 b, `import-smoke`, `deploycheck.greet`); 1 real Python skill (`csv-sum`). This is why the classifier unwraps rather than refuses: refusing malformed bodies would have kept two genuine documents dead, which is the thing E.SK1 set out to fix. |
| Built | `skills::classify_skill(&Skill) -> SkillBody` — `Capability { tool, spec }` / `Code { lang, source }` / `Instructions { text }` — called by BOTH executors. `lang_from_str` is deleted, not patched: its only caller was this bug. A double-encoded body is unwrapped once before it is judged. `run_code_skill` and `run_capability_skill` join `run_instruction_skill` so all three runners are shared, each stating its own precondition instead of the arm guessing on their behalf. `parse_run_skill` returns `(name, target)`. The 33-line capability branch left unreachable in the tool arm was removed rather than left to rot. |
| Also fixed | `handle_skills` opened with `let sb = self.sandbox.as_ref()?;` — the whole handler, listing and searching and document runs included, died at the door on a box with no interpreter. Following prose needs no interpreter. The requirement moved to `run_code_skill`, where code actually runs. |
| KILL criteria — held | (1) No path guesses an interpreter: an undeclared language is prose, asserted over `md`, `capability`, `org-mode` and empty. (2) Both directions tested at the seam a user reaches — a document never reaches `run_python`, declared source is never read aloud to a model. (3) One classifier, both executors. (4) A capability with no target says what it needs and names the phrasing that supplies it. |
| Actual metric | mind-conversation 434/0 (+3), workspace 1042/0 (42 crates). |
| Not claimed | That the documents produce good work — E.SK1's disclaimer, unchanged. And NOT that every phrasing works: the save-skill parser fires on any sentence containing both `save` and `skill`, which is how my first live attempt was swallowed and answered with "Run something green first". Real, reproducible, and deliberately left alone rather than fixed in the same change. |
| Residual | `headroom-check` is Python mislabelled `capability`, so it now classifies as a document and its source will be read to a model as instructions. Honest and non-destructive, but wrong — the fix is re-banking it with `lang: "python"`, which is a data repair, not a code change. |

## E.SK3 - the document runs, and cannot work: an instruction executor with no tools - pre-registered

*E.SK2's live verification passed on every criterion it set and still produced two refusals. The classification was the bug I went looking for; this is the one the evidence handed me afterwards.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| LIVE evidence, from E.SK2's own verification | `run skill test-market: check WMT` — a 6,149-byte research document, `runs=0` for its entire existence — returned a receipt instantly, ran on the board as `kind: skill`, and delivered: *"I cannot perform this task. I do not have access to real-time market data, live news feeds, or financial databases. My instructions strictly prohibit using model-internal knowledge."* `headroom-check` delivered: *"I can't run that script. I have no shell or network access, and I won't pretend I fetched live usage data."* Both are honest, correct, and useless. |
| What makes it certain | The SAME document, run through the `research` delegation path, produced real numbers — job `89ef07`: *"WMT closed at $105.38 on Aug 25, 2026, down 1.04%... market cap $838.62B, PE 38.19."* The document is fine. Its executor is not. |
| The defect | `instruction_steps` builds `Think { prompt } -> Notify { result }`. A bare model call, no tools. Two feet away, `delegate_cmd` runs `self.researcher.run(&task)` — a web-capable agent that returns `{answer, sources}` and writes its sources to scratch. E.SK1 chose the recipe because `import_agent`'s SCHEDULED path used it, and I reused that recipe without asking whether a scheduled document had ever produced anything either. The one thing I never checked is the one that mattered: **whether the executor could do the work the documents describe.** |
| Shape of the change | When a researcher is configured, an instruction document runs through it — the composed instructions plus the run's input ARE a task — with sources written to scratch as the research path already does. The bare recipe stays as the fallback for a box with no researcher, so nothing loses an executor it had. |
| KILL criteria | (1) A document losing the job-board row, the receipt, or the scratch trace — E.SK1/E.SK2's guarantees are not renegotiable. (2) The two executors diverging in what they record. (3) A box without a researcher losing the ability to run documents at all. |
| THE RESIDUAL THIS DOES NOT FIX, named because it is the more serious one | `record_skill_outcome(name, out.ok)` records that the EXECUTOR completed, not that the TASK was accomplished. So both refusals above were banked as successes: `test-market` now reads **1 run / 1 success**, `headroom-check` **6 / 2** — its deliverable an explicit refusal to pretend. For `csv-sum`, `ok` means exit 0, a real proxy. For a document, `ok` means the model emitted text. The skill ledger now mixes two meanings of the word under one column, and selection reads that column. This is a milder strain of the disease E.SK2 fixed — nothing is fabricated, the executor really did run — but the number overstates, and I am recording it rather than letting a green count stand for it. Separating them needs a judge on the deliverable, which is a slice of its own and NOT claimed here. |
| LIVE result | `4f83c39` deployed. The SAME sentence, `run skill test-market: check WMT`, job `c88e16`: receipt instant, deliverable in 2m18s — a full ticker report in the format the 6,149-byte document specifies. `**$105.38** | -$1.11 (-1.04%)`, day range, 52W range, market cap $838.62B, P/E 38.19, forward P/E 35.24, dividend $0.99 (0.94%), analyst consensus Buy (43 analysts), avg target $128.43, and four news items with attribution and a "why it matters" line each. Fields it could not source read `N/A` rather than being invented. |
| The flight trace, from scratch | `trace: skill:test-market:c88e16` → `task: check WMT` → `following banked instructions (research)` → seven sources (Google Finance, Yahoo, Investing.com, MSN Money, StockAnalysis, Benzinga, Walmart IR) → the deliverable. Everything E.SK1 and E.SK2 guaranteed survived the executor swap: the board row, the instant receipt, the trace, and now the sources the research path always wrote. |
| KILL criteria — held | (1) Row, receipt and trace all present, verified in `jobs json`. (2) Both executors record through the same `(ok, msg)` pair and the same two calls. (3) A box with no researcher keeps the recipe; with neither, it is refused before a row — tested, since a bare test engine has neither. |
| The residual, now with a number | `test-market` reads **2 runs / 2 successes**. The first of those two deliverables was *"I cannot perform this task."* The counter cannot tell them apart, because `ok` means the executor completed. Stated in advance, and left standing as measured rather than quietly rounded. |
| Caught in my own patch, before it shipped | The fallback branch had the composed prompt in hand and handed it back to `instruction_steps`, which wrapped a SECOND "Follow these standing instructions" preamble around the first. Invisible in a diff and it changes what the model reads. `instruction_steps_from_prompt` passes a ready prompt through untouched, and a test asserts the doubling it exists to prevent — including that re-wrapping really does double, so the guard cannot quietly stop meaning anything. |
| Actual metric | mind-conversation 436/0 (+2), workspace 1044/0 (42 crates). |
| Decision | **KEEP**, with the outcome-semantics residual carried forward as the next candidate slice. |

## E.SEC1b - the audit must certify production's policy, and production's policy panics - pre-registered

*Codex's review of E.SEC1 (`fbd5d2d`, `9b0540a`): provisionally KEEP at the detector/test gate, corpus REMAINS CLOSED, six points to resolve before final acceptance. This entry answers them. Point 3 was answered by the code before I had written a line of the fix.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| POINT 3, ANSWERED FIRST, AND IT IS A LIVE PANIC | Codex asked me to prove malformed or multibyte text cannot panic span handling. It can. `first_sensitive` does `text.to_lowercase()`, finds offsets in the LOWERED copy, and slices the ORIGINAL with them. `to_lowercase` is not length-preserving — `İ` (U+0130, 2 bytes) lowers to 3 — so every offset after such a character is shifted, and a shifted offset can land inside a multibyte character. `first_sensitive("İpassword日本")` → **`byte index 11 is not a char boundary; it is inside '日'`** at safety.rs:283. Reproduced before any change. |
| Why that is serious rather than tidy | The panicking call is reachable from `gate_write` (mind-memory:187), which scans arbitrary user text on every memory write. A panic in the gate is a denial of the write path, and this is exactly the bug class already recorded against the extractor: find on the lowered copy, slice the original. I fixed it there and reintroduced it here. |
| Fix | `to_ascii_lowercase()`. All markers and phrases are ASCII, so ASCII-only lowering matches identically, and it is length- and boundary-preserving by construction: non-ASCII bytes are untouched. Plus a char-boundary floor on the `value_follows` window, which slices at a fixed 48 bytes. |
| POINT 1 - canonical policy | Correct: `tools/sec1_audit.py` reimplements classification, so it can certify a policy production does not run. Replaced by a Rust audit binary that calls `mind_types` directly. The Python is DELETED rather than kept as orchestration, because its only remaining job would be the classification it must not do. |
| What replaces its value, because deleting a second implementation costs something real | The Python twin earned its keep once: it found the 28 chain-hash false positives. That value is converted into two things that cannot drift — permanent Rust fixtures for the cases it found, and a SENSITIVITY CONTROL in the audit binary itself. The audit runs known-sensitive canaries and known-clean strings BEFORE it scans anything; if a canary is not flagged it exits **2 = INCONCLUSIVE** rather than reporting a clean bill of health. This is the test rule already adopted with yantrikdb-core: a negative result must demonstrate its own sensitivity, in the same run, on the same build. An instrument that cannot fail is the same defect as a detector that cannot fire. |
| POINT 2 - structured credentials | `{"password":"hunter2"}` is already caught (the phrase is followed by a plausible value across the JSON punctuation). `{"api_key":"..."}` is NOT: `api_key` is absent from the phrase list, and a value-only walk sees a key with no context and a value with no name. Adding `mind_types::sensitive_pair(key, value)` so the KEY's semantics and its SCALAR value are judged together, in the shared crate — the walk stays in the audit, the classification does not. |
| POINT 4 - boundary proof | The four boundaries, named and each to be exercised by a test against the SHARED detector: memory write `mind-memory/src/lib.rs:187`, observability redaction `mind-observability/src/lib.rs:183`, governance egress `mind-governance/src/egress.rs:291` and `lib.rs:201`, eval export `mind-evals/src/coverage_oracle.rs:488`. Each test asserts BOTH that the boundary refuses/redacts AND that its error or log output carries no part of the input. |
| POINT 6 - artifact fail-closed | A rendered export artifact is scanned with the canonical detector before it may leave, and refuses on any finding, reporting counts and kinds only. |
| POINT 5 - historical findings | Its own entry (E.SEC1c), as Codex asked. Not folded in here. The two RIDs stay unread. |
| KILL criteria | (1) Any panic reachable from a detector boundary on arbitrary bytes — the audit's own corpus is adversarial input. (2) The audit reaching a verdict from anything other than production's detector. (3) An audit that reports "clean" without having proven, in the same run, that it can report "dirty". (4) Any finding, log line or error message that carries a matched value. (5) A widened detector that stops catching what E.SEC1's fixtures already catch — the two pre-existing tests that calibrated the thresholds stay authoritative. |
| Not claimed | That the detector is complete. It is a floor with named shapes, not a classifier of all secrets. |

## E.SK4 - a delegation named after a skill was only a label - pre-registered

*Pranab: "why doesn't it work from the desktop app? i tried with test-market. same results." It is not the desktop app.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| What I checked first, and what it ruled out | The service is on `4f83c39`, so E.SK3 is live. The console I verified through is `8077`, bound to `127.0.0.1` only; the desktop app reaches `8078` (`YM_CHAT_PORT`), a member-scoped surface. Different port, different identity - and NOT the cause: both call `conv.turn` → `handle_turn_as`, and both of that function's `handle_skills` call sites are reachable regardless of identity. The surfaces converge. |
| The actual cause | `delegate_cmd` parses `<name>: <task>` and then routes on the TASK alone. The NAME is never looked up as a banked skill. So `delegate test-market: check WMT` starts a generic research job labelled "test-market" and the 6,149-byte document is never opened. Every prior `test-market · research` row on the job board is that: jobs `983bf2` and `89ef07` researched the bare string "check WMT" and produced a decent answer that owes nothing to the document. |
| Why I did not see it | I verified E.SK1-3 through `run skill …`, the phrase path. `delegate …` is a different verb into a different function, and it is the verb Pranab actually uses because it is the one the desktop app's board is built around. E.SK1 named this exact companion and deferred it as "optional": *"delegate_cmd matching a delegation name against a banked skill, so `delegate test-market: check WMT` runs the document."* It was never optional - it is the form the user types. The same lesson as E.SK2, one verb over: I keep fixing the path I test instead of the path that is used. |
| Shape of the change | In `delegate_cmd`, before routing on the task: if the name EXACTLY matches a banked skill, run that skill through the shared classifier and the same three runners the phrase path and the tool arm use, with the task as its input. Exact match only - a fuzzy match would hijack delegations that merely resemble a skill name. |
| KILL criteria | (1) A fourth executor, or any classification decided here rather than by `classify_skill`. (2) A delegation whose name is NOT a banked skill behaving any differently than it does today. (3) A skill run this way losing the board row, the receipt or the scratch trace. |
| Not claimed | That every delegation should consult the skill bank. Only that an exact name match is an unambiguous instruction, and that answering it with a generic researcher was the mind ignoring what it had been given. |
| LIVE result | `51ade2c` deployed. `delegate test-market: check WMT` → job `e6d5b4`, **kind `skill`** rather than `research`, receipt instant, deliverable in ~2m: the document's own report format. The verb Pranab types now reaches the document the name refers to. |
| Actual metric | mind-conversation 437/0 (+1), workspace 1059/0 (42 crates) with `--locked`. |
| Decision | **KEEP.** |

## E.SEC1b - results

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Point 3 | FIXED and proven. `to_ascii_lowercase()` plus a char-boundary floor on the value window. Tested by weaving `İ ı ẞ U+0307 日 🔑 BOM é Ⱥ` through eight bodies at every byte boundary, asserting no panic AND that every reported span is sliceable — a span that cannot be sliced was computed against something else. The test corrected my own expectation once: `İpassword日本` is correctly `None` (no value follows), not a finding. |
| Point 1 | `tools/sec1_audit.py` DELETED; `crates/mind-evals/src/bin/sec1_audit.rs` classifies through `mind_types` only. Sensitivity control runs first — 6 canaries must flag, 5 clean controls must pass — and exits **2 = INCONCLUSIVE** rather than certifying anything if it fails. |
| Point 2 | `sensitive_pair(key, value)`. Live demonstration on a synthetic corpus: `{"api_key":"9f2b1c4d8e"}` is flagged at `event.api_key (key+value)`, while `{"password_policy":"requires 12 characters"}` passes. |
| Point 4 | Four named tests, one per boundary, each with a control proving it is not simply closed: `mind-memory::sec1b_boundary`, `mind-observability::sec1b_boundary`, `mind-governance::sec1b_boundary`, `mind-evals::sec1b_boundary`. Each asserts the boundary acts AND that its output carries no part of the input. |
| Point 6 | `scan_export_artifact` + `render_for_witness_checked` / `render_cases_checked`. There is no other function returning the rendered string. |
| THE AUDIT FOUND A DETECTOR FALSE POSITIVE, on the box, in production data | Three `event.confidence` values in `mind.db.decisions.jsonl` were reported as payment cards. A confidence float's fractional part is a long digit run that can begin with 3-6 and satisfy Luhn by chance — `0.5500005555555559` does. Reachable from `gate_write`, so a memory carrying a precise number would have been refused as a credit card: the "asian food recipes" failure wearing arithmetic. `is_embedded` now also rejects a run that is the fractional part of a decimal; only a point WITH A DIGIT ON ITS FAR SIDE counts, so `I paid with 5500005555555559.` ending a sentence is still a card. **Identified from key path and span length alone. No value was read, printed or transmitted.** |
| Why the deleted twin saw none of it | A COVERAGE difference, not a classification one. Its DecisionEvent scan enumerated a fixed field list — goal, outcome, predicted, lesson, trigger, chosen, subject, and four arrays — which does not include `confidence`. The canonical walk visits every scalar. The twin agreed with production on everything it looked at; it looked at less. The lesson is not that the second implementation was wrong: **a scan's field list is part of its policy, and an audit that enumerates fields certifies only the fields it enumerated.** |
| Read-only audit, canonical detector, after the fix | `mind.db.decisions.jsonl` 224 lines / 0 flagged · `egress_receipts.jsonl` 635 / 0 · `mind.db.read_receipts.jsonl` 12,006 / 0 · `tape.jsonl` 411 / 0 · `mind.db` 78 memories / **2 flagged credential-phrase**, the same two RIDs as before, span_len 6, still unread. `--fail-closed` exits 1. |
| Actual metric | Workspace 1059/0 across 42 crates with `--locked`. |
| Still CLOSED | The recorder-grown corpus. No export until Codex's explicit GO. |

## E.SEC1c - remediation of two historical findings - pre-registered, NOT executed

*Codex point 5, as its own entry. Nothing here has been run.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| The findings | Two `mind.db` memories, RIDs `01a00bd3-ed38-7322-979b-cc7bc1ea491f` and `01a00bd9-dd71-72c8-be8e-607d770508c7`, both `credential-phrase`, span_len 6. Found by the audit on 2026-08-26 and re-confirmed by the canonical detector. **Neither has been read, printed, exported or transmitted, by me or by any tool I ran.** They are known by identifier and kind only. |
| Plan, in order | (a) BACKUP first — a copy of `mind.db` and every hash-chained log, with the chain head recorded before and after, so the pre-remediation state is recoverable and provably unmodified. (b) QUARANTINE BY IDENTIFIER — a deny-list of RIDs consulted at retrieval and at export, so the two rows stop reaching any reader without their bytes being touched. (c) PRESERVE the chain — no in-place rewrite of any hash-chained record, ever; the logs are append-only and a rewrite would break the property that makes them evidence. (d) HUMAN CLASSIFICATION LATER, locally — Pranab decides what they are, on the box, with no content leaving it. |
| Why exclusion rather than deletion | Deletion destroys the evidence needed to judge whether the write gate should have caught them, and the write gate is the thing under review. Exclusion is reversible and provable; deletion is neither. |
| KILL criteria | (1) Any read, print, export or transmission of either memory's content before a human has classified it. (2) Any in-place edit of a hash-chained record. (3) Remediation running before a verified backup exists. (4) A quarantine that hides the rows from the AUDIT as well as from readers — the audit must keep seeing them, or the next run reports a clean bill of health it has not earned. |
| Status | Pre-registered only. Awaiting Codex's read and Pranab's decision. |

## E.SK5 - an API error was banked as a deliverable - closed

*Pranab pasted a live run: `📥 [test-market] (sub-agent synthesis error: OpenAI-compatible API request failed)` followed by six real NVDA sources.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| What happened | The researcher searched six pages successfully, then SYNTHESIS failed. `AgentResult` has no failure signal, so the error was formatted INTO `answer`: `format!("(sub-agent synthesis error: {e})")`. Callers decided success with `!res.answer.trim().is_empty()` - and that string is not empty. So the job board showed **done**, the error message was presented as the deliverable, and `record_skill_outcome` credited the skill with a success. |
| The residual I had NAMED, arriving as its worst case | E.SK3 recorded that `ok` means "the executor completed", not "the task was accomplished", and I judged it a milder strain. It is not milder. A refusal at least says something true; this banked an API outage as a finished report. |
| Root cause, one level up | A FAILURE DISGUISED AS CONTENT. Every consumer had to guess from prose whether it was reading a report or an error, and three of them guessed the same wrong way: the instruction runner, `delegate_cmd`'s research path (which hardcoded `"done"`), and the courier. |
| Fix | `AgentResult.error: Option<String>` and `AgentResult::ok()` - the one place callers ask. All four construction sites set it, including the two that already stringified errors. Consumers now ask the field. The answer stays human-readable on failure and the SOURCES still go out, because they are what the run did accomplish: the difference between "it broke" and "it broke after reading six pages, here they are". |
| Actual metric | mind-agents 75/0 (+1), workspace 1062/0 with `--locked`. |

## E.SEC2 - the privacy guard was switched off by a line break - PARTIALLY closed, decision needed

*Found because E.SK5's edit happened to join a wrapped method chain onto one line, and the guard fired for the first time.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| The guard | `privacy_audit::no_new_unscoped_inference_calls` exists because an unscoped `inference.chat()` takes the household (cloud) lane silently and never touches `PRIVACY_ESCALATED` - so a private-carrying call written as a bare `chat()` leaks AND reads as clean on the dashboard. Its own docs cite the DMN leak and `work_radar_run` shipping 40 verbatim user messages to a cloud provider on a daily timer while the counter read zero. |
| The blindness | It matched the literal `inference.chat(` **one line at a time**. rustfmt wraps the chain as `.inference` / `.chat(...)`, so no single line contains the pattern. Every wrapped call was invisible. A guard a line break can switch off is not a guard. |
| Scale | **19 unscoped calls across 12 files**, hidden for as long as the guard has existed. Among them: `mail.rs` x3 (email header triage, sender classification from aggregates, email analysis), `finance.rs` (recurring subscriptions FROM EMAIL METADATA), `calendar.rs`, `briefing.rs`, `onboarding.rs` x2, `studio.rs` x3, `lib.rs` x5, `foresight.rs`, `plugins_mod.rs`, `skills.rs`. |
| Fixed now | The scan strips line comments, removes ALL whitespace, then matches - wrapping-proof, and a `//` mid-chain cannot split it either. `chat_grounded(`/`chat_scoped(` still do not match, since the character after `chat` is `_`. Pinned by a test that asserts a wrapped chain matches and the grounded forms do not, so the blindness cannot return silently. |
| Fixed now (the worst one) | `mind-agents` sub-agent SYNTHESIS moved to `chat_grounded`. Its sibling decision loop 120 lines earlier was already grounded, with a comment saying a sub-agent's task carries household context - and the synthesis prompt carries the task AND every observation gathered, strictly more. The inconsistency was invisible for the same reason as the rest. |
| NOT fixed, and deliberately not | The other 19. This box HAS a private lane (`YM_PRIVATE_PROVIDERS=ollama-local`, qwen3.8:27b), so `chat_grounded` would move mail triage, finance parsing and the briefing off `deepseek-v4-pro` onto the local model AND fail closed when that endpoint is down. That is a confidentiality-versus-capability trade on features in daily use - Pranab's call, not the auditor's. |
| How the backlog is held honestly | A SEPARATE `UNSCOPED_PENDING` list, never folded into `UNSCOPED_ALLOWED`. The allowlist means "we looked, and this prompt cannot carry household memory". The pending list means "we looked, and it probably CAN - a human has to choose". Merging them would record a privacy decision nobody made, which is worse than the original blindness because it would look settled. A test asserts every pending file STILL has an unscoped call, so the list can only shrink toward empty and cannot become permission. |
| KILL criteria for the remainder | (1) Any pending entry surviving without a decision once Pranab has read this. (2) Any of them moved to `UNSCOPED_ALLOWED` without a stated reason the prompt cannot carry household memory. (3) The wrapping-proof scan being narrowed to make a failure go away. |
| Actual metric | Workspace 1062/0 across 42 crates with `--locked`. |

## E.SEC3 - the same offset bug, in the parsers a user actually types into - closed

*I proved span safety for the DETECTOR when Codex asked (E.SEC1b) and stopped there. A sweep I ran at the time flagged the same pattern in the chat-path parsers and I did not follow it up. This closes that.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Reproduced BEFORE the fix, on the live chat path | `parse_run_skill("İ run skill 日本")` → **`byte index 14 is not a char boundary; it is inside '日'`** (skills.rs:91). `parse_save_skill("İ save that as skill 日本")` → the same at skills.rs:67. Both reachable from any message a user types, in the console or the desktop app. |
| The quieter half, which is worse | Where it does NOT panic it corrupts silently. `parse_run_skill("İ run skill csv-sum")` returned **`Some(("sv-sum", ""))`** — the name lost its first character, so the lookup fails with "No skill named sv-sum" and the user is told their skill does not exist. A panic gets reported; this gets shrugged at. |
| Cause, for the third time today | `to_lowercase()` is not length-preserving (`İ` U+0130 is 2 bytes and lowers to 3). Offsets found in the lowered copy were used to slice the ORIGINAL. Fixed in `mind_types::first_sensitive` this morning as E.SEC1b; the same shape was sitting in eleven bindings in `skills.rs` and five in the `lib.rs` compose cluster, whose offsets slice `text` (or, in four cases, slice `l` from a `low` built out of it). |
| Fix | `to_ascii_lowercase()` at all sixteen. Every pattern these parsers search for is an ASCII command word, so ASCII-only lowering matches identically — and it is length- and boundary-preserving by construction. Left alone deliberately: `suggest_skill`'s `hay`/`q`, which lowercase for word MATCHING and never index, and where folding non-ASCII case is a feature. |
| Pinned | Every parser run against seven boundary-breaking characters (`İ ẞ ı U+0307 日 🔑 é`) inserted at every byte position of seven real command sentences, asserting no panic — plus the exact corruption case asserted to return `csv-sum`, not `sv-sum`. |
| AND the ambiguity in the same file | `parse_save_skill` tested `l.contains("save") && l.contains("skill")`, which is true of "run the SAVEd SKILL named X" — and save is dispatched ahead of run, so asking to RUN a banked document was answered with *"Run something green first."* **This is what swallowed my own first live attempt to run `test-market`, and I mistook it for a routing bug at the time.** `save` must now be a whole token (`word_offset`) and must appear BEFORE the marker it applies to. Controls assert the four real save phrasings still parse, so the trade is not a regression. |
| KILL criteria | (1) Any parser on the turn path panicking on arbitrary user text. (2) A parse that silently changes what the user asked for. (3) A save request no longer being heard — hence the controls. |
| Actual metric | mind-conversation 441/0 (+2), workspace 1064/0 (42 crates) with `--locked`. |
| The pattern worth keeping | This is the third distinct place today the same defect lived: the detector, these parsers, and (in a different form) the privacy guard's line-at-a-time scan. All three are the same mistake — **reasoning about a transformed copy of a string as though it were the original**. When one turns up, the sweep is not optional; I did the sweep, saw the hits, and moved on anyway. |

## E.SEC4 - finishing the sweep, and the bug that was not about unicode at all - closed

*E.SEC3 ended with "when a sweep returns hits, finish the sweep". I had swept two files. This sweeps all 42 crates.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| The instrument | A script over every `.rs` file in `crates/`: for each `let X = Y.to_lowercase()`, report whether the same scope slices `Y[`. Deliberately over-inclusive - each hit read by hand. **19 candidates.** After the fixes below, 2 remain, both benign (`is_confirmation` / `is_denial`, which compare and never slice). |
| THE FIND, and it is not a unicode bug | `research.rs` built `let l = text.trim().to_lowercase()` and then cut the topic out of the **untrimmed** `text`. Leading whitespace shifts every offset by exactly its own length. Proven before the fix: `"  research quantum computing"` → topic **`"h quantum computing"`**; `"\n\nlook into the pack lease bug"` → topic **`"o the pack lease bug"`**. Five call sites, all on the live turn path. |
| Why this one matters more than the panics | It needs no exotic input at all - a leading space or a message beginning on a newline, which is what a pasted request looks like. And it is SILENT: the mind does not fail, it goes away and researches the corrupted topic, then reports confidently on it. The unicode panics at least announce themselves. |
| Fix | Slice the SAME string the offsets come from: `let text = text.trim();` before `let l = text.to_ascii_lowercase();`. That closes both halves at once - the trim shift and the length-changing lowercase. |
| The rest of the sweep | `to_ascii_lowercase()` at every remaining binding whose offsets index an original: `courier.rs` x2, `mail.rs`, `onboarding.rs`, `lib.rs` x5, `research.rs` x2, and `mind-tools/src/asked.rs`. |
| A CORRECTION to what I said an hour ago | I reported the first sweep as "all in mind-conversation - meaningful in itself: no other crate does this kind of text parsing." That was wrong. My line-distance heuristic hid two real hits, one of them in `mind-tools/src/asked.rs`, where an offset into a lowered copy slices `line`. The tidy conclusion was an artefact of the instrument, and I stated it as a finding. |
| Pinned | Every touched parser run against `İ ẞ U+0307 日 🔑` inserted at every byte position of five real sentences, plus the whitespace cases asserted to return the whole topic for `""`, `" "`, `"  "`, `"\n"`, `"\n\n"`, `"\t"` and `" \n \t "`. |
| KILL criteria | (1) Any offset derived from one string used to index another that is not byte-identical to it. (2) A parse that silently changes what the user asked for. (3) The sweep script being loosened to make a hit disappear. |
| Actual metric | mind-conversation 443/0 (+2), workspace 1066/0 (42 crates) with `--locked`. |
| The class, stated once | **Four defects today were the same mistake: reasoning about a transformed copy of a string as though it were the original.** `first_sensitive` (lowered copy → panic in `gate_write`), the skill parsers (lowered copy → panic and silent name corruption), `privacy_audit` (the guard matched one line at a time while rustfmt wrapped the chain → 19 unscoped cloud calls invisible), and this one (trimmed copy → silently corrupted research topics). The transform differs; the mistake does not. |

## E.SEC4 - finishing the sweep, and the bug that was not about unicode at all - closed

*E.SEC3 ended with "when a sweep returns hits, finish the sweep". I had swept two files. This sweeps all 42 crates.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| The instrument | A script over every `.rs` file in `crates/`: for each `let X = Y.to_lowercase()`, report whether the same scope slices `Y[`. Deliberately over-inclusive - each hit read by hand. **19 candidates.** After the fixes below, 2 remain, both benign (`is_confirmation` / `is_denial`, which compare and never slice). |
| THE FIND, and it is not a unicode bug | `research.rs` built `let l = text.trim().to_lowercase()` and then cut the topic out of the **untrimmed** `text`. Leading whitespace shifts every offset by exactly its own length. Proven before the fix: `"  research quantum computing"` → topic **`"h quantum computing"`**; `"\n\nlook into the pack lease bug"` → topic **`"o the pack lease bug"`**. Five call sites, all on the live turn path. |
| Why this one matters more than the panics | It needs no exotic input at all - a leading space or a message beginning on a newline, which is what a pasted request looks like. And it is SILENT: the mind does not fail, it goes away and researches the corrupted topic, then reports confidently on it. The unicode panics at least announce themselves. |
| Fix | Slice the SAME string the offsets come from: `let text = text.trim();` before `let l = text.to_ascii_lowercase();`. That closes both halves at once - the trim shift and the length-changing lowercase. |
| The rest of the sweep | `to_ascii_lowercase()` at every remaining binding whose offsets index an original: `courier.rs` x2, `mail.rs`, `onboarding.rs`, `lib.rs` x5, `research.rs` x2, and `mind-tools/src/asked.rs`. |
| A CORRECTION to what I said an hour ago | I reported the first sweep as "all in mind-conversation - meaningful in itself: no other crate does this kind of text parsing." That was wrong. My line-distance heuristic hid two real hits, one of them in `mind-tools/src/asked.rs`, where an offset into a lowered copy slices `line`. The tidy conclusion was an artefact of the instrument, and I stated it as a finding. |
| Pinned | Every touched parser run against `İ ẞ U+0307 日 🔑` inserted at every byte position of five real sentences, plus the whitespace cases asserted to return the whole topic for `""`, `" "`, `"  "`, `"\n"`, `"\n\n"`, `"\t"` and `" \n \t "`. |
| KILL criteria | (1) Any offset derived from one string used to index another that is not byte-identical to it. (2) A parse that silently changes what the user asked for. (3) The sweep script being loosened to make a hit disappear. |
| Actual metric | mind-conversation 443/0 (+2), workspace 1066/0 (42 crates) with `--locked`. |
| The class, stated once | **Four defects today were the same mistake: reasoning about a transformed copy of a string as though it were the original.** `first_sensitive` (lowered copy → panic in `gate_write`), the skill parsers (lowered copy → panic and silent name corruption), `privacy_audit` (the guard matched one line at a time while rustfmt wrapped the chain → 19 unscoped cloud calls invisible), and this one (trimmed copy → silently corrupted research topics). The transform differs; the mistake does not. |

| The sweep became a STANDING GUARD | `crates/mind-conversation/src/source_audit.rs`, modelled on `privacy_audit`: it scans every crate, function by function, for a binding lowered from a variable whose offsets then index that variable. A script is a thing someone has to remember to run - and I ran one, saw the hits, and moved on while it still had them. As a test, a new occurrence fails the build. `ALLOWED` is EMPTY and should stay so; an entry is a claim that the transform is length-preserving for every input the function can receive. A second test asserts the check FIRES on the shape it exists for and does NOT fire on `to_ascii_lowercase` or on a lowered copy that is only compared - an instrument that cannot fail is the same defect as a detector that cannot fire. |
| The guard's own two false-positive classes, both found by using it | (1) `let category = toks.iter().map(..).join(" ").to_lowercase()` lowercases the JOINED STRING, not `toks`, so pairing it with a `toks[i]` Vec index is nonsense - sixteen of those buried the real hits. Fixed by accepting only a plain variable wearing identity-preserving calls (`trim`, `as_str`), with `trim` deliberately INCLUDED because it shifts offsets too. (2) Matching the bare substring `t[` finds it inside `next["id"]`, and `s[` inside `parts[0]`. **That is the same substring-for-token mistake that made `contains("save")` fire on "saved" - this time in the instrument rather than in the code it audits.** Fixed with a token-boundary check. |
| Final metric | mind-conversation 445/0 (+4), workspace 1068/0 (42 crates) with `--locked`. Sweep: 19 candidates → 0 real, guard green. |

## E.P5a - one reliability verdict, replacing four expressions of it - pre-registered

*The first half of ARCH-6 P.5. Written before the code, and against the code rather than the plan: two of the plan's claims did not survive contact.*

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| The defect | One policy - "failing often enough to stop trusting" - is written FOUR times, in three different shapes. `mind-memory/src/lib.rs:3152` as an **SQL predicate** (`runs>=4 AND (successes*2) < runs`); `mind-conversation/src/surface.rs:750` as a Rust bool (`s.runs >= 4 && s.successes * 2 < s.runs`); `mind-agents/src/procedure.rs:69` as a match on a `Prior` (`Basis::Measured { runs } if runs >= 4 && value < 0.5`); and `mind-spec:81` disagrees outright - `Prior::is_trustworthy` uses `runs >= 5` where every other site uses 4. |
| Two corrections to ARCH6's own P.5 description | (1) It names a fourth copy at `pack.rs:476`. **There is no such site** - the file has no reliability rule.
